THE FIGURE ONE HUNDRED AND FIVE IN THIS BULLET UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 WAS NOT REPRODUCIBLE BY MEASUREMENT AND THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0005 MEASURED IT AND GOT ONE HUNDRED, AND THE MEASUREMENT WINS; THE BATCH 0005 REVIEW'S OWN REMARK THAT THIS FIGURE AND THE FIFTY-SEVEN BELOW SHOULD BE RE-MEASURED RATHER THAN COPIED FORWARD IS THE REASON BOTH ARE CORRECTED HERE.** The three batches before it read ninety-eight, eighty-eight and eighty-four, so this batch is above all three and the reason is printed here rather than hidden: **Chapter 239 prints one long subtraction in front of a bank and the running figure in it is *nine hundred and ninety-eight thousand four hundred*, and that phrase is printed twelve times in one chapter, and *two hundred and forty-nine thousand six hundred* is printed four more, and neither can be abbreviated without making the arithmetic uncheckable.** Fifteen of the twenty-five stand inside that arithmetic and are load-bearing. The avoidable ones were cut by varying *about nine people* into *about seven people* and *about half of us*, and *nine years* into *since before the water* and *since I came*, and *about nine men* into *about nine or ten hands* and *a cart-yardful of men*, and *nine minutes* into *four minutes*. **The load-bearing nines in this batch and the reason each stands:** the nine pounds a roof; the nine shillings a week; the nine hundred and sixty pence that is four pounds; the nine days to Orison and the nine days' board; nine carts a day for about a hundred and forty days, which is the second line of the four-page order and the whole of the Anner's road cost; the nine districts the chamber prints for; a table nine feet by fourteen; the nine feet between the Anner's sill and the frame; nine printed sheets in a bag; nine feet of the sill; **the nine people in a valley of four hundred and ten who are frightened of a bench, and about nine things in this basin that are true in every room and absent from every page, and about nine men in a cart yard with a road in chalk.** Those last three are different nines of different things and the volume does not stop to say so, and the clerk of forty-four's joke about a number in a room at `227:62` is the reason it does not have to.
- **The word ELEVEN stands FIFTY-SIX TIMES over Chapters 231–240**, and that is a count of occurrences and not of anything in the story. It stood at sixty in the first writing and three came out with the "nine men" variations. **THE FIGURE FIFTY-SEVEN IN THIS BULLET UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 WAS NOT REPRODUCIBLE BY MEASUREMENT AND THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0005 MEASURED IT AND GOT FIFTY-SIX, AND THE MEASUREMENT WINS.** The load-bearing elevens: the eleven seats, of which eight have never had anybody in them; the eleven hours the eleven hands took; the eleven people who built the settlement; the eleven hundred tons this basin says it trades; the eleven miles of no road on the north side of the Anner. **The figure FORTY-FIVE appears nowhere in this batch**, and neither does the forty-five times comparison, and the percentage is used instead.
- **The stock pause *a minute and a half* returns ZERO over Chapters 231–240.** It stands once in the whole of Volume 05, at `209:25`, and none was in this batch's first writing. **THE MECHANICAL LAYER, AND EVERY FIGURE IN THIS BULLET WAS RE-MEASURED OVER THE CHAPTER FILES BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF 26 SEPTEMBER 2026 BECAUSE THE FIRST PRINT OF IT WAS WRONG IN THREE PLACES. *about nine words* stands at THREE over the ten chapters and not at TWO, and the three are `234:53` (*in about nine words*), `234:77` (*It was nine words and it was this*) and `236:31` (*in nine words*); the first print named `234:79` and `236:33`, and NEITHER OF THOSE LINES CONTAINS THE PHRASE — `234:79` is *A roof does not care and neither do I* and `236:33` is *Their good name is public and it is free*, and both of those are nine words long and NEITHER IS COUNTED BY THE INSTRUMENT, because a sentence being nine words long and a sentence saying *nine words* are two different things and the first print conflated them. A fourth instance, *in about nine words* at `236:37`, was in the delivered text and is out, and the recordwright's line there now reads *in one sentence*.** *grind ink* returns ZERO in this batch, and it returns SEVEN over Chapters 201–240, at `211:51`, `214:65`, `219:53`, `226:19`, `227:39`, `228:36` and `230:23`, and the Batch 0003 block names only three of the seven and is wrong about the other four. **Paragraph-initial *Nobody* stands at TWENTY-FOUR over the ten chapters — 2, 2, 4, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3 — and it stood at THIRTY-TWO in the delivered text, nine above Batch 0003 and five above Batch 0002, and the first print of this bullet called that residue *a deliberate tic* and said it should be told to a Volume 06 writer rather than left to find. IT IS NOT A DELIBERATE TIC. AGENTS.md's repetition bar is unconditional and a narrator who opens thirty-two paragraphs of ten chapters with one word has a habit, not a voice, and the repair cut eight of them by changing the sentence rather than the word: *Not one of them argued with him about it*, *Not one person in this basin has a figure for that road*, *The second one went past that room without a word for a long time*, *There was nobody on the roof*, *Nothing else was said about it in that kitchen*, *Not one person in that lane stopped her*, *There was nothing said for a while*, and *It is not on the record that he is a good man*. The remaining twenty-four are this volume's ordinary voice and a Volume 06 writer is told the number and not a justification.** **AND THE HEDGE, WHICH THE REVIEW NAMED AND WHICH NO BULLET IN THIS BLOCK HAD COUNTED: the word *about* stands TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO TIMES over the ten chapters, and it stood at THREE HUNDRED AND TWENTY in the delivered text, with FORTY-EIGHT of them in Chapter 234 alone and forty-six in Chapter 236. It is now 25, 26, 21, 27, 29, 32, 29, 28, 12 and 23, and the worst chapter is thirty-two. Chapter 239 carries twelve because Chapter 239 prints an arithmetic and a hedge inside an arithmetic is a lie about the arithmetic. Twenty-one percent of the hedges were cut and none of the cuts touched a figure, a date or a a finding.** **The pause *as long as it takes to* stands at FOUR, at `231:23`, `232:23`, `233:23` and `239:31`, and it stood at eight; *turn a cup round on a board* stands at ONE, at `232:23`, and it stood at three; *about four minutes* stands at FOUR and it stood at seven. All three are the same tic wearing three coats and the repair left one of each standing where it was least tired.**

### 3. THE THREE CLOCKS, unchanged, obeyed in every chapter, and the engine of the volume is still arithmetic and not politics

**The Shelf keeps a grain year of fifty-two weeks. The Red Basin keeps three clocks and there is no conversion between any of them, and the finding that there is no conversion was made ONCE, at `202:45`, in a salt-pan's mouth, and forty-eight chapters are about what people do about a thing that has already been found out.** The pan-book year: fifty saltings, more or less, a salting being a drying, a dry-year salting about seven weeks and a wet-year one eleven, and therefore no length in a salting and no first drying that holds twice. The turn: a year from the first shaking, which is turn nought, twelve years back, and this year is turn twelve. The day: a day since the second shaking, out of the water-book, and it is the only date in this basin that can be put on the outside of a bag that is going nine days to a city of four hundred thousand people.

**AND WHAT THIS BATCH DID TO THE CLOCKS IS SMALL AND IT IS THE POINT. The basin put a turn on a piece of paper for the first time in this batch and could not place it.** The chamber's own return that came down in Chapter 235 is dated in the eleventh year, and the return under the fourth column is dated in the eighth year, and **the chamber's own printed index has, under the same heading, a return of the water at the frame taken in the eleventh year, and the only date anybody in this valley can put on any of it is the day it came back, out of the water-book, in a hand of seventeen.** And the man of thirty-one said out loud at `239:61` that a turn cannot be placed in this basin by anybody living, and that therefore the only date this valley can put on the whole of a sum is the morning it was said out loud on a bank, and that he did not put that morning on it either, **because a man may put a day on his own act and may not put one on another person's speech, and what he did that evening was write a sum down.**

**AND THE SHELF SIDE STAYS SHUT. The Shelf's grain year is fifty-two weeks, week fifty-two is days eighty-four to ninety, and therefore Shelf day ninety-one is the first day of a NEW grain year on the Shelf. This batch does not turn the year, does not say the year turns, does not put the four hundred and eighty-five pounds into a Red Basin's mouth, and does not open the Shelf half of a penny exercise book once in ten chapters.** `240:17` names the figure in a list of things that do not stop, in his own hand's ledger, and says it is in a county four hundred miles from this valley, and the observation is Batch 0003's and is the volume close's to decide.

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed in a script and the working printed beside it. **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE SAME ONE EVERY TIME: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply.**

- **A roof, wet through, is about £9 to put back, and it is NOT broken into thatch and battens and nails, and the man who does roofs is asked to split it in this batch and refuses.** £9 = 2,160d ✓ (`231:3`).
- **Nineteen roofs at nine pounds is £171 exactly, and eighteen of them are wanted after the first.** 2,160d × 19 = 41,040d; 41,040 ÷ 240 = 171 with no remainder ✓ (`239:23`).
- **THE RECURRING FIGURE, and it is the number the batch's last arithmetic is about: roofs 41,040d + the whole wage 1,728d + the second hand 112d = 42,880d = £178 13s 4d a year.** 16 × 108d = 1,728d; 112d for 112 days; 41,040 + 1,728 + 112 = 42,880d; 42,880 − 42,720 = 160d; 160 ÷ 12 = 13s 4d ✓ (`236:47`, `238:41`, `239:23`).
- **THE MATTER TO ORISON, and it is NOT SPENT IN THIS BATCH. Entry a pound, the day four pounds, nine days' board at eightpence.** 240d + 960d + 72d = **1,272d = £5 6s** ✓; and two of them is 2,544d = **£10 12s** ✓. Printed in full with the working at `234:39`, and the chapter that prints it is the chapter that says there is no price on the list for asking for a thing about thirty-two papers and that nobody in this valley has five pounds and six to put into a wall.
- **THE NEW FIGURE OF THIS BATCH, AND IT IS THE BATCH'S REAL DISCOVERY: THE CHAMBER'S OWN TONNAGE FOR THIS BASIN, IN THE EIGHTH YEAR, WHICH IS FOUR YEARS BACK, IS ONE THOUSAND AND FORTY TONS, and this basin has been saying about eleven hundred tons for nineteen years.** 1,040 × 240d = **249,600d to the pound** ✓; at £4 a ton at the mouth, 4 × 249,600 = **998,400d = £4,160** ✓; at £11 a ton at the market, 11 × 249,600 = **2,745,600d = £11,440** ✓; the difference of £7 a ton, 7 × 249,600 = **1,747,200d = £7,280** ✓. And the difference between the two tonnages is **60 tons**, and at seven pounds a ton that is **£420 a year**, which is 100,800d out of 1,747,200d, and 100,800 × 100 = 10,080,000 ÷ 1,747,200 = **5.77, five and seven-tenths in the hundred** ✓, and nobody in this basin can say where sixty tons went and the clerk of the Seat says so out loud and says he is not going to be the one who says it (`235:31`, `235:33`, `239:11`).
- **THE SUM, and it is the finding, and it is one subtraction in front of a bank printed with the working in the mouth of a man who counts carts (`239:33`).** 42,880d + 998,400d = **1,041,280d**; 240 × 4,338 = 1,041,120d, remainder 160d = 13s 4d, so **£4,338 13s 4d** ✓. The whole of what the Anner takes out of this basin in a year is what a ton is worth at the mouth, **£4,160 = 998,400d**. 1,041,280d − 998,400d = **42,880d = £178 13s 4d**.
- **AND THE EXCESS IS EXACTLY THE SETTLEMENT, to the penny, and the chapter says so and then says the man who worked it out is not going to be the one who says what it means (`239:37`, `239:41`, `239:51`).** The ceiling, printed beside it: 998,400d − 42,880d = **955,520d**; 240 × 3,981 = 955,440d, remainder 80d = 6s 8d, so **£3,981 6s 8d** ✓ (`239:47`). And the nine men in the cart yard came in at £4,160, which is £178 13s 4d over the ceiling, **because £4,160 is the only figure in that yard anybody could name.**
- **WHAT THE SETTLEMENT IS AS A SHARE OF THE ROAD'S MONEY, on this basin's own figure of about eleven hundred tons: two and three-tenths in the hundred** (`236:47`). £7,700 = 1,848,000d; 42,880 × 100 = 4,288,000; 4,288,000 ÷ 1,848,000 = **2.32** ✓.
- **THE ROAD COST THAT IS RESTATED AND NOT RE-DERIVED, and it has no figure on it in any document in this basin: about nine carts a day for about a hundred and forty days on a road that adds a morning and a half each way, and the Anner bears the whole of it (`232:5`, `238:13`, `238:43`).** A morning and a half each way is about three hours out and three back, and nobody in this valley has ever put a figure on it, and the figure that exists is in a pit's book and not in a room.
- **AND THE FIGURES THAT TRAVEL AS DEBTS AND NOT AS PRICES, AND NONE OF THEM IS NAMED IN A RED BASIN'S MOUTH IN THIS BATCH, WHICH IS A DECISION AND NOT AN OMISSION.** Four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year on nine thousand seven hundred; the second notice of five shillings and fourpence; the claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence; the ninety-one numbered sheets; the box of about nine hundred and forty forms; the minute of the first of the forty-eighth week; the dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty and his wife. Check: 9,700 × 12d = 116,400d = £485 ✓; 4 × 16d = 64d = 5s 4d ✓; 406 × 32d − 2,160d = 10,832d = £45 2s 8d ✓. **All seven are named at `239:63` and `240:17`, in a list of things that are not stopping, and `239:63` says the figure is in a county four hundred miles from this valley and was not mentioned once in a valley that has just worked out what a second road costs. Nesh Arrad's letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived and no chapter of this batch says that it has.**

### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, once each, with the chapter and the line

1. **A DOCUMENT IN THIS VALLEY IS USED RATHER THAN OBERVED, for the first time in this volume, and the use is that a man of thirty-four cannot read the copy he keeps and reads it out at the closing of the water because it is the only way anybody in that lane finds out whether a thing has been done** (`231:37`, `231:41`).
2. **THE READING DID NOT HAPPEN ON ONE DAY, because the copy was up the hill being copied, and about eleven people came down a lane to a road at the closing of the water and there was nothing to read, and the man of thirty-four stood in it and said *I have not got it*, and that failure is what produced the finding** (`231:19`).
3. **IT IS NOT A CEREMONY. IT IS A PROMISE ABOUT A DAY IS WHAT EVERYBODY THOUGHT IT WAS, and the finding is the man of thirty-four's and not the man of thirty-one's, and it is that a settlement of work and days in a valley of four hundred and ten people is administered by one man saying it out loud in a road, and that is a civic instrument nobody in this basin has a word for** (`231:37`).
4. **A PAPER KEPT IN A LANE BY A MAN WHO CANNOT READ IT HAS NO SECOND COPY, and the woman of thirty-eight said it in a road four miles up a hill in the plainest sentence anybody said that day, and she named the reader's own nine words of the week before as the reason she was not going to tell him he was doing it wrong** (`231:49`).
5. **AND THE COPY WAS NEVER ONCE IN HIS HANDS IN THE WHOLE FIFTY-FOURTH WEEK, and the man of thirty-one asked for it to be read out and it was read out and the whole of his instrument was what a man of thirty-four said in a road** (`231:53`).
6. **THE TRADESMEN OF THE BASIN PRICE A ROUTE THAT DOES NOT GO THROUGH ORISON, and it is named once in a cart yard on the fifth day of the fifty-fourth week by a man who counts carts, and it is not followed, and the chapter says out loud that it is the shape of the next book in this valley and that nothing else in that week was about it** (`232:11`, `232:49`).
7. **THERE IS NO GATE ON IT. That is the whole of it, and every trade in this basin has carried salt and ochre past the gate at the top of the pass for eleven years and not one of them has ever asked what is at the other end of it, and the answer is a room with a yard in it and about thirty clerks in that building and a bench of four above them** (`232:25`).
8. **THE FIRST PERSON IN THIS VOLUME SAYS OUT LOUD THAT THE WATER-SEAT IS THE REASON THERE IS A SEAT AND NOT THE OTHER WAY ROUND, in a cart yard, in the mouth of a woman of thirty-three who copies deeds for a penny a page, and the whole of her argument is that there is a room because there is a press and the press is because a bond of forty pounds is in it, and that the water has been brought to that room every year since the turn before and nobody in this basin has ever gone the other way** (`232:37`).
9. **THE COLUMN IS NAMED A SECOND TIME IN THIS VOLUME, IN AN ORDINARY MOUTH, BY THE PERSON WHO HAS TO LOOK AT THE HEADING EVERY DAY.** The heading is two words and the second of them is the ordinary word a clerk writes at the head of a column of things that did not go, and a clerk of forty-four said it the way he says a price, to a book, in a room with seven people in it (`233:29`, `233:31`, `233:33`).
10. **BLOCK-QUOTED DOCUMENT ONE, AND THE BATCH'S ONLY ONE: the back of the last page of the Seat's own hand-book, opened at the last page and held open by a finger, a printed heading of two words, about four hundred and sixty entries in the three columns beside it going back to the first shaking in a small hard hand, one rule under all four, and nothing whatever under the fourth** (`233:29`–`31`).
11. **AND THE REASON THE HAND-COPY IS EMPTY AND THE ORIGINAL IS NOT, and it is the volume's own method and not a conspiracy: a hand-copy of what was sent has nothing to copy, and the material under a column headed *not sent* is exactly the material that was not sent** (`233:53`).
12. **FOUR LINES UNDER THAT HEADING AND ALL FOUR OF THEM ARE ABOUT THIS BASIN**, described in prose and not block-quoted: a return of the water in the Sallow at the frame, taken in the eleventh year, with a figure in it that nobody in this valley has ever seen; a list of the papers this Seat has sent to the chamber, being forty-one, and of the answers, being nine; a sheet of the tonnage and the prices of the trade of this basin in the eighth year; and a printed notice of one line which is the rule the first of the three rulings was made under (`233:57`).
13. **THIRTY-TWO PAPERS WENT NINE DAYS UP A ROAD AND DID NOT COME BACK AND NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY HAS EVER BEEN TOLD, and it is the second of the four lines, and it took a clerk of forty-four about four minutes to say and about a minute longer to say the part that matters, which is that nobody has hidden anything and that not being sent is a worse thing for us because nobody did it** (`233:69`, `233:73`).
14. **NOBODY ASKS, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO OUT LOUD, and it lists the five people in that room who could have asked and did not, by name and by reason, and the fifth is a man of thirty-one who could have opened it and did not and put his hand on the table about a foot from a printed sheet and took it away again** (`233:37`, `233:39`, `233:47`).
15. **THE BASIN'S ANSWER TO A RECORD THAT WAS NOT SENT TO IT IS TO GO ON DOING THE WORK, and that is neither indifference nor heroism, and it is the plainest thing about this valley in about a month, and it is the batch's subject** (`234:23`).
16. **BEING AFRAID IS NOT A MATTER, and a clerk of forty-four said it and said he has looked for a place to enter nine people being frightened of a bench and there is not one in this basin, and said the reason, which is that they are not frightened of anything they have done and a person cannot be entered anywhere for a thing they have not done** (`236:29`).
17. **THE FIRST PERSON OF THE RED BASIN TO ASK HIM A DIRECT QUESTION ABOUT A THING HE IS CARRYING, and it is a man of fifty-six with a ladder on his shoulder at the bottom of his own roof, and the answer is nine words and it is not helped, and what he got was *Then you will carry it home, and I have four roofs*** (`234:63`, `234:65`).
18. **A ROOF DOES NOT CARE AND NEITHER DO I — nine words, and it ends the argument in the lane, and the finding is the roofer's and not the protagonist's, and he is not going to be the man who brings a bench to a lane, and the reason is that a roof does not care and he has a roof to put on in a frost** (`234:77`, `234:79`).
19. **A THING THAT IS TRUE IN EVERY ROOM AND ABSENT FROM EVERY PAGE IS NOT A CONSPIRACY AND IS NOT AN ERROR, IT IS A THING THAT HAS NO OWNER, and a woman of thirty-eight said it in a lane in the ordinary way while not being asked and not setting out to say anything, and there are about nine of them in this basin and she counted them twice and is not counting them a third time, and one of them is a height, and nobody in that lane asked her which nine** (`235:65`, `235:69`, `235:71`).
20. **AND THE ONLY FIGURE ANYBODY ANYWHERE HAS EVER WRITTEN DOWN ABOUT WHAT THIS VALLEY TRADES, and it was written four years ago four hundred miles off by a man who has never been in the Red Basin, and it is one thousand and forty tons where this basin has been saying about eleven hundred for nineteen years, and the difference is sixty tons and nobody can say where sixty tons went** (`235:29`, `235:31`, `235:33`).
21. **A MINE IS NOW A BODY WITH A STANDING INTEREST IN A NUMBER, and the under-manager said it and then said *not the height* twice before anybody could get it wrong, and what he meant is that a number a body pays every year to keep true is a number that body is frightened of losing** (`235:41`, `235:43`).
22. **THERE IS NOW NOWHERE IN THIS BASIN TO PUT A NUMBER A PERSON WROTE THEMSELVES, and a girl of seventeen found it, in her own hand, in her own book, off a frame at the closing of the water, and the water-book has four columns and they belong to four hands and not one of the four has ever stood at the frame** (`235:75`, `235:79`).
23. **NINE PEOPLE IN A VALLEY OF FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN ARE AFRAID OF A BENCH OF FOUR THEY HAVE NEVER MET, and four of them said so in four places in one day in four different voices and none of the four would use the word** (`236:3`).
24. **THEIR GOOD NAME IS PUBLIC AND IT IS FREE — nine words, said by an under-manager of fifty-two at the end of a day he was not asked for, and he is right, and being right is the whole of the danger, and a recordwright of twenty-eight named the reason on the way out in nine words: a public thing that costs nothing cannot be spent by the people it is about** (`236:33`, `236:37`).
25. **THE ANNER'S STANDING INTEREST IN ELEVEN FEET STOPS BEING AN ARITHMETIC FACT AND BECOMES A THING A MAN OF FIFTY-TWO HAS TO THINK ABOUT AT ABOUT FOUR IN THE MORNING, in a house at the top of a hill and not in the chain-book, and he does not write it down, and he goes down to the dam, and nobody in that chapter has said that he is a good man and nobody has said he is not** (`236:53`).
26. **A NAME TRAVELS FOUR MILES IN ABOUT TWO YEARS ON ABOUT FOUR MOUTHS AND NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY SPENT NINE DAYS CARRYING IT AND NOBODY WAS PAID ANYTHING FOR IT AND NOBODY PUT IT IN A BOOK, and there is nothing anybody in this basin could produce if anybody wanted to argue about it, and about four people would say the name and three of them could not say where they had it** (`237:45`, `237:51`, `237:53`).
27. **THE COLUMN POINT WAS MADE A MONTH AGO AND IS NOT BEING MADE AGAIN, and what is new in this batch is only that the name has started to move on its own, and nobody went looking for her, and he has not asked whether she knows about a column with four lines in it that are all about this basin, and nobody in this basin has told him or anybody else whether she knows** (`237:45`, `237:61`).
28. **THE BATCH'S REVERSAL, AND NOBODY IS A VILLAIN IN IT AND IT IS NOT A FLOOD. A carter took one load over the shoulder on the second of that week because a customer is four days short of Orison and the shoulder is two days shorter for that one place, and he was right about all three of the load, the road and the two days, and about eleven men in a cart yard have had that road in chalk since the fifth of the week before and not one of them is a bad man and not one of them is a good man, they are men who count carts** (`238:3`, `238:45`).
29. **A PIECE OF PAPER THAT FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN PEOPLE HAVE NEVER READ ALL THE WAY THROUGH DEPENDS ON WHERE A CART GOES, and the woman of about forty read the second line of it out loud in a lane at the closing of the water because a carter asked her a question about a cart, and she did not change a word of it, and a carter at the back said *that is not a river, that is a schedule*** (`238:13`, `238:19`, `238:25`, `238:31`).
30. **THE ELEVEN FEET AND THE HIGH ROAD ARE THE SAME QUESTION AND NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY HAD PUT THEM TOGETHER, and the under-manager said it on a shut causeway in the wind in about four minutes to about seven people on a bank, and he said his name and his age and that he had been under-manager for nineteen years and that he was going down to the dam now because the clay was waiting, and that this was the sixth time in a month that a thing in this basin had turned out to be a road** (`238:57`, `238:59`).
31. **THE SUM OF THE SETTLEMENT OF THIS VALLEY AND THE COST OF A SECOND ROAD IS OVER THE WHOLE OF WHAT THE ANNER TAKES OUT OF THIS BASIN IN A YEAR BY EXACTLY THE SETTLEMENT, and the working is printed four times in a chapter and a man who counts carts says out loud that there is nothing behind that difference and that he is not going to be the one who says what it means and that whoever is minded should decide it not to be him in a room with a table in it** (`239:33`, `239:37`, `239:41`, `239:51`).
32. **A BOARD WITH A FIGURE ON IT WENT UP AGAINST THE OUTSIDE OF A CART SHED, and it is going to go in about a month, and the man who chalked it knows that and did it anyway, and about seven people walked past it and two of them stopped and nobody in Hollow Sallow has asked what it is for** (`239:57`, `239:67`).
33. **THE SYSTEM PUT NOTHING UP AND SAID NOTHING WHATEVER ABOUT ANY OF IT, and a man of thirty-one has stopped expecting it to, and he did not tell anybody that he had stopped expecting it, and there is no instrument in this basin for anybody to notice it in him** (`240:25`).
34. **AND NOBODY WENT DOWN, and the position is where it was identified in the fifty-first week, and four hundred and six feet is the pit and not the void and the void is more than that by a quantity nobody in this basin has an instrument for naming, and the last hundred and forty feet of the water at the low end of the pit has been under to the roof since before a woman of twenty-six was seventeen, and nobody in this valley said any of it out loud this week and nobody thought about it this week** (`240:29`, `240:31`).
35. **SOMEBODY IS GOING TO OPEN THAT COLUMN AND IT IS NOT GOING TO BE ME, in the plainest sentence anybody said that week, said by a clerk of forty-four to a shelf, and nobody answered it and nobody was asked, and it is the last thing in the batch and the thing Batch 0005 is for** (`240:45`).
36. **AND THE BATCH ENDS ON THREE ORDINARY THINGS AND NONE OF THEM IS A RULING: a gate opened at the closing of the water, a chair put back at a table, and a bag put on a shelf that nobody has asked about** (`240:49`).

### 6. THE DEVICES, and none advanced

- **The leg. CANON and carried: the finding it rests on is `155:7` — the leg goes when he is carrying a thing somebody else is going to have to read, and it comes back when the carrying stops. IN THIS BATCH IT DID NOT COME BACK, and that is a consequence of the carrying and not a rule and not a cure.** It is used **ONCE, at `234:59`**, in the mouth of a man of fifty-six who does roofs, as a fact and not as a discovery, and the reason is not given and he has not told one person in this valley. **IT WAS IN TWELVE CHAPTERS' WORTH OF DRAFTING AND IT WAS IN 233 AS WELL, and it was taken out of 233 in this pass, because one use in a batch is the rule and two is a refrain. NO FILE AND NO CHAPTER IN VOLUME 05 STATES A COUNT OF DAYS SINCE THE LEG CLEARED, and none does.**
- **The rendering. Not staged, referred to or hinted at in this batch.** Volume 04's last word on it is *not to be referred to again*.
- **The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture, the token and the afterimage: none is mentioned in this batch and none is advanced.** The token is not touched and the fourth use stands: it took the words.
- **The continuity line is not refused again and no twelfth refusal is permitted, and it is not on the Red Basin's table.** Volume 04 refused it eleven times. It is not refused here and it is not paid off.
- **THE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ IS NOT PRINTED IN THIS PROJECT, in any file, in any figure, and it is not printed in this block, and this batch adds nothing to it in the fiction.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. **It is exercised three times in ten chapters and it is a real constraint every time and it is never a disclosure and never a count: the copy in the lane was never once in his hands in the whole of the fifty-fourth week; the hand-book at 233 is in a clerk's hands and is read out by that clerk and never handed over, and the chamber's printed index is on a table and he put his hand about a foot from it and took it away again; and the man of thirty-four who keeps the copy in the lane still cannot read it and still reads it out, and in Chapter 231 the finding is that the copy has no second copy and the reader IS the copy, and nobody in that lane has ever worked out why a man can do that.**
- **AND THE ONE DEVICE THIS BATCH SPENDS IS SPENT HERE, once, and may not be restaged: A DOCUMENT ADMINISTERED BY BEING READ ALOUD BY THE PERSON IT IS ABOUT, WHERE THE PERSON CANNOT READ IT AND THE COPY IS THE ONLY ONE THERE IS, so that the administration of a settlement of work and days is a man saying in a road what a paper said, and getting it right twice and not being checked (`231`).** **It is not the 228 device and it is close to it, and the difference is the whole of it: in Chapter 228 a man who cannot read amended the paper in the reading and a document acquired a line; in Chapter 231 the amendment is not the subject and the FAILURE to have the paper in his hand is, and nobody changes a word.**
- **AND THE TEN UNREPAIRED META-NARRATION SENTENCES IN CHAPTERS 201 TO 210, of which `205:48` names a chapter number in the prose, and the inherited defect at `207:67`, are recorded and not repaired and were not built on. This batch produced five of the same class in its own first writing and all five were caught by the same scan. See section 12.**

HIS NAME IS NOT ON THE ELEVEN-HAND SHEET AND THE PLACE WHERE IT WAS IS NOT FILLED AND NOT DASHED, THE HEIGHT IS NOT ON THE SHEET, AND NO CHAPTER OF BATCH 0005 MAY PUT ANY OF THOSE THREE THINGS BACK.**

A woman of about fifty-four making bread and a man of about forty-two in a cart yard are named in Chapter 237 and are people of the Red Basin who were in a house the whole time and not people founded in order to found a route.**

AND THE NINTH, SPENT IN CHAPTER 231: a document administered by being read aloud by the person it is about, where the copy is the only one there is and the reader is the copy.**

**`state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and is three volumes stale: it reads `currentPhase: batch-0002`, Volume 01, Chapters 11–20, status `planned`, attempts 0. **AND THE MEASURED FACT ABOUT THE DISPATCHER THAT THE PREVIOUS BLOCK RECORDED, RE-MEASURED AND UNCHANGED: `find workspace -name PROMPT.md | sort` puts `workspace/continuation/next-0001` FIRST of the unclosed directories, and that directory is the phase that wrote Chapters 221 to 230 and it has no `.done` marker. ### 11. WHAT THIS BATCH OWES THE VOLUME, AND WHAT IT DID NOT PAY, and the hand-off

**PAID:** the first time a document in this valley is used rather than obeyed, and the finding that the reading is the administration and that the copy has no second copy; the tradesmen of the basin pricing a route that does not go through Orison, named once and not followed; **the column named twice, in an ordinary mouth, by the person who has to look at the heading every day; the material under it found to be about this basin and not sent to this basin, in four lines, of which one is a rule this basin already has on a wall; thirty-two papers that went nine days up a road and never came back; the only figure anybody anywhere has ever written down about what this valley trades, and sixty tons nobody can account for**; the first person of the Red Basin to ask him a direct question about a thing he is carrying; **a roofer of fifty-six ending the argument in nine words and a finding that is his and not the protagonist's**; nine people in a valley of four hundred and ten frightened of a bench they have never met, and a recordwright naming why a public thing that costs nothing cannot be spent by the people it is about; Sable Venn's name travelling about four miles on an ordinary day with no document under it and nobody going to look for her; **the batch's reversal, done by the basin's own tradesmen for a good reason, with nobody a villain in it and a cart not where it was;** and the arithmetic of a road, in which the excess of the claims over the whole is exactly the settlement and there is nothing behind the difference.

**NOT PAID, AND NOT TO BE PAID IN A LATER BATCH OF THIS VOLUME BY ACCIDENT:** **the opening of the column, which is Batch 0005's climax at Chapter 245 or 246 and is the whole reason this batch names it and does not touch it; Sable Venn arriving; the revocable local charter; the exit review; the stone; the first missing anchor position, which was identified in Chapter 209 and is not moved; and the height.**

**AND THE LINE THIS BLOCK WILL NOT LET A LATER PASS CROSS: THE BATCH NAMES A THING AND DOES NOT TOUCH IT. Nobody asks for the column, nobody opens it, nobody is asked about it, and nobody asks him when he is going to, and a clerk of forty-four has said out loud in a room that somebody is going to open it and it is not going to be him, and nobody answered him and nobody was asked. The settlement stays on the wall with no name at the foot of it and no height in it and a seventh line that exists only in a lane. The route exists in about nine mouths and no money is behind it, and the money that would be behind it is more than the whole of the trade. The Anner is to be asked again in one year and the asking is a year off. A cart is not where it was and nobody in this valley can say whether the mine is paying for eight carts a day or nine. About nine people know something and can do nothing with it and are not relieved and are not to be relieved in a later batch by an act of kindness. And the second half of the volume's question is not asked out loud.**

**THE HAND-OFF IS UNCHANGED AND THIS BLOCK RESOLVES NOTHING, and the review repair of 26 September 2026 resolved nothing either. Every thread in the live block of `state/open-threads.md` is picked up whole except the sealed column, which this batch names twice and shuts. The new question is unanswered and is not compressed into one word, and the one sentence in the delivered text that named it is out, and the repair did not put a gesture in its place. **The batch's tenth and last chapter now ends on the same three beats it ended on and the middle one has a table leg in it.** The carrying is still off nothing. The blank is a blank. Nesh Arrad's letter has not arrived and no pass may say that it has. Nobody is redeemed. Nobody is forgiven. **And the next phase is Volume 05 Batch 0005, Chapters 241 to 250, the redacted archive and a rule-free alternative, and its prompt is the one this phase writes and is at `workspace/volume-05/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`.**

### 12. THE THINGS THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS, ALL CORRECTED IN THE PROSE BEFORE DELIVERY, AND ALL RECORDED HERE BECAUSE A LATER PASS NEEDS TO KNOW THE SCANS RUN AND ARE NOT CLEAN ON THE FIRST PASS

1. **ONE WEEKDAY.** The word *Tuesday*, spoken by the under-manager in the first writing of Chapter 235 in a sentence that was reaching for the stock phrase a man in a lane says when he has not been asked something. **Caught by the plural-safe weekday pattern, which is the pattern the Volume 04 audit found this project was structurally incapable of running. It is now *It is not a complaint about that sheet, and I have sat with it for an hour to find out whether it is, and it is not.* This is the second weekday in three batches of Volume 05 and the guardrail is not the problem and the scan is late.**
2. **FIVE META-NARRATION SENTENCES**, at `233:39`, `234:41`, `235:79`, `236:59` and `237:45`. All out. **The cause is the chapter card's frame and not a slip: this batch's cards said *the finding is* eleven times and the prose caught five of them. A Batch 0005 writer should write the cards in the frame *what happens* and not *the finding is*, and the standing joke inside the fiction about the volume's own vocabulary does not protect a narrator that refers to itself.**
3. **SEVEN BOLD MARKERS**, of which one was a bold heading inside the block-quoted document in Chapter 233 and six were emphasis in running prose and dialogue in Chapters 231 and 236. All out. **A bold scan over the ten files now returns ZERO, and the emphasis count is ZERO, and the block quote is block-quoted and not bold.**
4. **TWO ITALICISED SPOKEN PHRASES** at `231:27` and `236:37`, which are emphasis with a different marker and are now in quotation marks. **Seven italic spans remain and every one of them is a phrase quoted back out of something written.**
5. **A LINE NUMBER IN THE PROSE.** The first writing of Chapter 236 cited `227:15` in a sentence about a woman of about thirty-one with two children. It is now *she is one of the nineteen, and there is a sentence of hers on the settlement under a strike that she did not put there for this and has not been asked about.* **A chapter may not cite a chapter, and the narrator knows nothing about a chapter.**
6. **A WRONG DATE INSIDE A LEGAL BAND**, at `237:47`: *On the sixth day of the fifty-second week* for an event of the fourth day of the fifty-first week, which is Chapter 210. **The day number was legal and the week was in its band and the date was wrong, and the pattern cannot see it, and the third question caught it.**
7. **A COUNT IN THE LANE.** The first writing of Chapter 234 said *the way about four hundred and six people in this valley passed it*, and Hollow Sallow is about four hundred and ten people and the basin is not a village. Cut. **A figure that belongs to a village must not be used for a valley.**
8. **A COUNT THAT DID NOT ADD UP BETWEEN TWO CHAPTERS.** The first writing of Chapter 234 said a clerk of forty-four had watched the fear happen *in three places* and Chapter 236 gives four people in four places. Now *in more places than I can count inside a week*.
9. **A GARBLED SUBTRACTION IN 234's money paragraph**, which read *a pound, four hundred and sixty pence for the two days*. **The working was wrong in the middle of a sentence nobody had checked, which is the exact class the money instrument exists for, and the correct working is now printed in the mouth of the clerk: 240 + 960 + 72 = 1,272d = £5 6s.**
10. **A WRONG PENNY FIGURE AND A WRONG PERCENTAGE IN CHAPTER 235.** The first writing said sixty tons is *a hundred and eight thousand pence*, which is 108,000d and not 100,800d, and the percentage that came out of it was 6.18 and not the 5.77 the chapter needed. **Both corrected and the working is now printed in the sentence, which is the fourth time in this volume that a figure has been wrong in a state-shaped sentence rather than in a chapter.**
11. **THE LEG IN TWO CHAPTERS INSTEAD OF ONE.** It was in Chapter 233 in the first writing, in a sentence about a man standing nearest to a printed sheet, and it is in Chapter 234 in a roofer's mouth. **Cut from 233 and the sentence now says the man had four hundred miles behind him and nothing whatever in his hands.** One use in a batch is the rule and two is a refrain.
12. **THE NAME NAMED FOUR TIMES INSTEAD OF TWICE.** The first writing of Chapter 237 had the string *Venn* four times in two mouths. **It is now twice, once from a woman of about fifty-four in a kitchen in Sallowby and once from a man of about forty-two in a cart yard at Hollow Sallow, and the two have never met and neither can say where he had it, and that is the permission in `outline/volume-05.md` and no more.**
13. **A FIRST-PERSON VERB IN A THIRD-PERSON NARRATION.** The first writing of Chapter 233 said the Seat *has paid for every one of them since I came*, in a narrator's sentence. **It is now *without once being asked to*, and a clerk of forty-four's own first person is kept inside his own speech.**
14. **A UNIT WRONG IN THE MIDDLE OF A DIVISION IN CHAPTER 239**: *Two hundred and forty pounds goes into that four thousand three hundred and thirty-eight times*, which is a division by pounds of a figure in pence. **It is now *two hundred and forty pence to the pound goes into that*, and the whole of Chapter 239 was then re-checked line by line against a script and every one of the twenty-one figures in it is right.**
15. **A NONSENSE FRACTION AND A MISCOUNTED INTERVAL IN CHAPTER 239**: *it is fourteen out of a hundred and eighty-seven*, which is not a statement about anything, and *the five days between the last one and this one*, where 187 − 183 = **3**. **It is now *that is a count of figures and not a count of anything else, and this is the hundred and eighty-seventh day, and the three days between the last figure and this one are not in the book and are not anywhere else.* 178 to 183 is **four** and `235:53` prints four, and 183 to 187 is three and `239:59` prints three.**
16. **THE WORD NINE AT ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-EIGHT**, against a volume trend of ninety-eight, eighty-eight and eighty-four. **Fifty-three cut, and the residue is explained in section 2 rather than hidden: fifteen of the twenty-five stand inside one subtraction in Chapter 239 and cannot be abbreviated without making the arithmetic uncheckable.**

### 13. TWO OBSERVATIONS THIS PHASE RECORDS AND DOES NOT EXPLOIT, BOTH CANON-ADJACENT, AND NEITHER IS THIS PHASE'S TO SETTLE

1. **THE SHELF'S GRAIN YEAR IS FIFTY-TWO WEEKS, THE FIFTY-SECOND WEEK IS DAYS EIGHTY-FOUR TO NINETY, AND THEREFORE SHELF DAY NINETY-ONE — WHICH IS CHAPTER 221 — IS THE FIRST DAY OF A NEW GRAIN YEAR ON THE SHELF, WHICH WOULD PUT THE FOUR HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-FIVE POUNDS DUE AT THE CLOSE OF THAT GRAIN YEAR AT THE DOOR OF BATCH 0003.** **This batch does not turn the year, does not say the year turns, does not put the figure into a Red Basin's mouth, and prints the debt only in a list of things that are not stopping, at `239:63` and `240:17`.** The observation is recorded so that the Volume 05 close, or a repair phase, can decide what a fifty-two-week year means against a day-count that has run to one hundred and eighty-eight, and the honest form of the decision is that it may need a sentence this volume has not earned yet.
2. **A COLUMN HEADED *NOT SENT* IS A DEVICE OF THE WHOLE PROJECT AND THIS VOLUME MAY WRITE EXACTLY ONE OF ITS FIVE FACES, AND THIS BATCH WROTE IT, AND THE VOLUME'S OWN PROOF THAT IT DID NOT WRITE IT AS A CONSPIRACY IS A CLERK SAYING *nobody has hidden anything* OUT LOUD IN A ROOM AND BEING ASKED ABOUT IT BY NOBODY** (`233:73`). **A later pass that wants to write a second face of this device — a redacted archive, a sealed file, a withheld return, a suppressed index — inside Volume 05 will find that this volume has already spent the one it is allowed and that the device the volume's climax needs is not a secret and never was.** The forty-one papers and the nine answers and the sixty tons are three holes in a record and not a hole in a wall, and the honest reading of the whole batch is that about nine people in a valley of four hundred and ten have learned that they are not being lied to and are not being told, and that neither of those is the same as being owed an answer.

This is the project's largest single drag and it compounds every phase that has to read these files. **It is not repairable inside a review pass, because the only safe prune is one a writer who has read the archive can justify line by line, and this repair has read the chapters and not the archive. THE RECOMMENDATION, WHICH IS NOT A FINDING AND IS NOT BINDING: a Volume 05 prune phase, before Batch 0005 is written, whose entire job is to move the four closed volumes out of the six live files and leave Volume 05 standing alone. It must not be done by a script and it must not be done by a phase that is also writing prose.**

**AND THE STRUCTURAL NOTE THE REVIEW LEFT WITH THE CONTROLLER, RECORDED HERE UNCHANGED IN SUBSTANCE.** The prose is competent, specific and well-voiced and the batch honours its plan. The risk is not rule-breaking; it is that Ilyan decides nothing across ten chapters, which is exactly what the batch prompt required of him and which means the Volume 05 climax at 245 or 246 — his opening the column — has no accumulated interiority to spend. `234:59`–`234:71` is the only place in the batch where he is treated as a person rather than a function, and it is nine words long. **A BATCH 0005 WRITER IS TOLD THIS HERE AND IS TOLD IT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN RECORD RATHER THAN BY A REVIEWER'S MEMORY: the batch that opens the column needs the man who opens it to have been somewhere in this batch, and the ten chapters he spent not deciding are not by themselves an accumulation. What this batch gave him is a shed with a door on the latch that a girl of seventeen has now asked about, and a sum on a board that is going in about a month, and neither of those is the column.**

### 1. THE MAP, one row per chapter, in both calendars. **AND THE FORMULA, which is the whole of the check: A BASIN DAY = THE SHELF DAY PLUS SEVENTY-EIGHT.**

Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. **The fifty-second week is days eighty-four to ninety, the fifty-third is ninety-one to ninety-seven, and the fifty-fourth is ninety-eight to one hundred and four.** Check both ends of the formula: 91 + 78 = **169**; 100 + 78 = **178**. The first two batches' formula holds at both of their ends too: 71 + 78 = 149; 80 + 78 = 158; 81 + 78 = 159; 90 + 78 = 168.

**THE DAY-PHRASE INSTRUMENT, run over Chapters 221–230 case-insensitively, returns FOURTEEN instances of the form `([a-z]+(?:-[a-z]+)?)\s+day of the ([a-z]+(?:-[a-z]+)?)\s+week`, standing on fourteen lines, and it returned TWELVE before the review repair of 26 September 2026, and the difference is `229:3`, which carried a garbled date of a form the pattern cannot match and now carries a legal one, and the count was miscounted at thirteen in the first print of this correction and is fourteen.** All fourteen name a day number of seven or less, none is of an illegal form, and every named week is inside its own Shelf band: the fiftieth is seventy to seventy-six, the fifty-first seventy-seven to eighty-three, the fifty-third ninety-one to ninety-seven, the fifty-fourth ninety-eight to one hundred and four. Three of the fourteen are references to an earlier day and not to the chapter's own day, and each of the three is now correct: `221:9` is the day the counting was begun, which is Shelf day seventy-one and basin day one hundred and forty-nine, and the same line's arithmetic is that a count begun on one hundred and forty-nine reads one hundred and sixty-nine twenty days later, which is the twenty days in that line; `222:51` is the fifth day of the fifty-first week, which is Shelf day eighty-one and basin day one hundred and fifty-nine, and it is Chapter 211's day, and the girl of seventeen read the four pages out to a neighbour with one eye on it (`211:21`, `211:29`); and `226:9` is the second day of that week, which is the day the draft was written and is Chapter 222's day. The other eleven are their own chapters' own days, at `221:53`, `224:3`, `226:3`, `226:67`, `227:3`, `228:3`, `228:13`, `229:3`, `230:3`, `230:9` and `230:33`. Compare Batch 0002, which returned twenty-six instances on twenty-four lines, Batch 0001, which returned eleven, and Volume 04, which returned 205 over fifty. The difference is the calendar, and it is a fact about two basins rather than about care.**

**AND THE INSTRUMENT IS BLIND IN A SECOND WAY, WHICH THE REVIEW OF THIS BATCH PROVED BY CERTIFYING A DATE THAT WAS WRONG, AND THE BLIND SPOT IS NOW NAMED. This paragraph above asked two questions of every instance it found — is the day number legal, and is the named week inside its own band — and BOTH ANSWERS WERE YES AT `222:51`, which named the second day of the fifty-first week for an event that happened on the fifth day of it. A THIRD QUESTION IS REQUIRED AND IT IS THE ONE NO REGEX CAN ASK: IS THE NAMED DAY THE DAY THE EVENT HAPPENED ON.** A day can be inside a legal band and still be the wrong day of that week, and the pattern cannot tell a Tuesday from the Thursday two days later because it does not know what happened. **The chapter's own arithmetic is what caught it, not the scan: the fourth day of the fifty-second week is Shelf day eighty-seven and the seventh is ninety, and eighty-seven plus eight is ninety-five, which is the fifth day of the fifty-third week, which is the day `225:3` is written on and the day its own sentence says it is.** **A LATER PASS IS TOLD HERE THAT A DAY-PHRASE SWEEP IS A LEGALITY CHECK AND NOT A TRUTH CHECK, and that the cheapest second instrument for this volume's calendar is to add every chapter's own day to every day-phrase in it and read the two lists side by side, which is ten rows against thirteen and takes a minute.**

The review of this batch counted the same ten chapters twice and reported the figure as having held flat against Batch 0002, and it did not hold flat: Batch 0002 stands at eighty-eight over ten chapters of the same length and this batch now stands four below it, and the direction of the change is the right one.** **Batch 0001's reviewed run stands at ninety-eight over ten chapters, so the three batches of this volume read ninety-eight, eighty-eight and eighty-four.** The load-bearing nines in this batch and the reason each stands: the nine pounds a roof; the nine shillings a week; the nine days' ride to Orison; nine times in eleven days and this was the tenth; the ninth door on the walk; the ninth turn; about nine people in a lane or on a bank or in a room; nine days of a sheet in a cart shed; and the nine words of the refusals that are estimates and not counts. IT STOOD AT SIXTY-FIVE BEFORE THE REVIEW REPAIR AND THE ONE THAT WENT WAS A FALSE COUNT AT `225:37`, and the review reported the figure as having held flat against Batch 0002's sixty-five. It did not hold flat in either direction: Batch 0002 stands at ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE over the same ten chapters of the same length, because that is the flood batch and the flood batch is the batch of eleven feet and eleven houses, and this batch is sixty below it.** The load-bearing elevens: the eleven houses, the eleven marks, the eleven feet, the eleven seats, the eleven men at the pit, the eleven shillings a week, the eleven hundred tons, the eleven years, the eleven days, the eleven days a sluice opens early, the eleven words, and **the eleven hands**, which is the batch. **TWO OF THE SIXTY-FIVE WERE CUT AS FILLER BEFORE DELIVERY: *about eleven minutes* at `224:3`, which is about ten, and *about eleven hours* at `227:3`, which was an echo of the eleven hands in the same paragraph and is now *the length of a working day*.** `227:62` is the volume's own joke about the number said out loud in a room, in a clerk's mouth, and it is the sentence that makes the rest of the sixty-five defensible.
