> **THE REPAIR PASS OF 26 SEPTEMBER 2026, AND A LATER PASS MUST NOT UNDO IT.** The Batch 0005 review found ten things and all ten are fixed. **Six were prose and four were record, and the batch was not restarted and no chapter was rewritten: every repair is inside a line that already existed, so all 834 chapter-and-line citations into Chapters 191–200 still point where they pointed.** The prose repairs, in full, so that a later pass knows they are deliberate: **196:7 said *the fourth of the forty-ninth week* and is now the fifth, the day 196:3 and the table both give; 192:35, 197:74 and 197:76 said *the thirty-fifth day of that month* and the Shelf has no months, and the three words are out, leaving a date that is canon at 162:99, 162:115 and 173:11; 197:80 dated the same set-aside to the third day of the forty-third week and 192:35 dates it to the thirty-fifth day, and 197:80 now says the thirty-fifth day; 198:3 said *the day after a draft went into a room four miles off* and the draft went in on the sixth day and the walk was on the sixth day, and it now says the same day, with the two facts about carrying dated to three days earlier, which is 194; 200:39 said *twice in nineteen years* where 193:11 has already made that letter the second, and it is now three times; and *Nobody in that room said anything* stood nine times in the batch and four times in Chapter 199's one scene, and three of the four and one in Chapter 195 are now other sentences, which leaves the bare sentence once in 199, once in 195, once in 193 and once in 191.** The record repairs are FINDING 16, the device ledger, `current.md:7`, `current.md:9` and the citation totals, and each is corrected at the site. **THE ONE THING THIS PASS DELIBERATELY DID NOT REPAIR IS `181:92`, which carries the same three words *of that month* and is Batch 0004's prose. It is flagged in `state/open-threads.md` and in the intervals table in `state/continuity.md` for the volume close to take or to record why it did not.**

### What this batch is

**A year of protection and a year of a man's memory turn out to be the same instrument, and a register cannot tell the difference between the two, and the batch's last finding is that the account's own book has a line in it that pays a year to somebody and does not say who.** The batch runs from the day the granted sitting was entered to the first day of the fiftieth week, and it takes up every thread that Chapter 190 left on the ground, and it answers the lease and pays the year and exposes the scheme and asks one question and answers nothing.

**There is no blight, no famine, no fight, no rescue, no monster, no new enemy and no romance beat. Nothing was won. Nobody is going to be ruined and nobody was redeemed and nothing was undone.**

### The five things that happened

- **A man who cannot read said so out loud before he signed, in a room four miles off, and the minute recorded it, and a chair said the thing nobody had ever said in that room: a room can use a person who cannot read what they have signed, once, and there is nothing whatever in the paper that stops a room using them again — and there is no other arrangement in the county and no form for one.** 191:27, 191:63, 191:71, 191:94. **And the minute's last line is the engine of the next six chapters: a witness is four persons of a district and the district has not appointed four (191:95).**
- **A year in this county is a unit of account and not a period of time, and it has been priced four times, and the four prices are dated and none of the four has ever been read out to the person it was charged to.** A reeve of fifty-four says all four at a beam in about four minutes, on the first day of the forty-ninth week, and then says the sentence the batch is built on: **a year costs whatever the thing is that somebody is paying, and the only person in this district who can tell you what a year of you is worth is a man who has been given a price and has not asked anybody whether it was fair** (192:17–35, 192:51). She will say the four prices four times a year and will not do it in a room and will not do it in a cut.
- **A witness is not four signatures, and it has got nothing to do with reading.** A carter of forty-six says it in about four seconds in a room with a cold grate in it: a witness is a person who has said a thing in their own words and can be asked about it afterwards, and three of the four of a bench have been using the word *reading* as a synonym for frightened (193:39). **And a convenor of sixty-one gives the reason he has never once in nineteen years asked the fourth one: that he did not want to find out (193:29) — and a woman of thirty-one at the bottom of a towpath says the thing nobody in that room can fix, which is that whatever the four of them turn out to be they are not going to be in a room on a day she is not going to (193:74).**
- **THE YEAR IS PAID, IN FULL, ONCE, IN CHAPTER 197, AND IT IS A SPECIFIC YEAR.** One year of his own memory out of thirty-one, the last of the years in which he was in a room with a person he had not yet met, and the reason it is the last one is in a book in the district and it is that the not was available and he took it. It goes in the ordinary way, in a room, before four sworn witnesses, in about nine minutes, and **nobody in that room can check what it was and that is not a trick.** Nobody relieves him of it, nobody says the price was fair, nobody tells him afterwards what was in it, and he says all three of those out loud in the room before he pays (197:47, 197:53, 197:55, 197:72). **The volume's fifth and last System panel is at 197:59–60 and it says nothing whatever about a year, and the rendering came up once in that room at 197:64 and he is in a bad way and nobody noticed, and the leg did not clear, because a year is not a thing anybody is carrying.** And afterwards he cannot say which year it was (197:100, 197:110) — **not the year in general, he can say what it was, in the same words; what he has lost is the year, and the reader knows the year and he does not, and that is the volume's last instrument.**
- **THE SCHEME IS EXPOSED, IN TERMS, WITH THE FIGURES, BY A MAN WHO DENIES NOTHING, AND HE SAYS THE NUMBER OUT LOUD HIMSELF.** Two sheets at a shilling and fourpence a sheet, two shillings and eightpence, the same in a hundred and one counties; nine hundred sent, three hundred and seventy named, five hundred and thirty blank or unreturned of which about one hundred and sixty never came back at all; about nine thousand in issue on the Shelf; three hundred and thirty-nine of the three hundred and seventy a man with one cart and thirty-one a man with more; a sheet valued at what it saves and what it saves is a load, and a load is three shillings on the register and two shillings and eightpence in that district's own book, and a sheet costs two shillings and eightpence, so the register's own valuation of a piece of paper in that district's book is the price of the paper and the load is not in it (199:19–27). **He gives up one office, the register itself, and says about nine thousand out loud himself and says the other nine thousand stay in issue and that he is not going to pretend otherwise (199:45, 199:49), and he is not redeemed and he says so in his own mouth, and he is not the last problem in the series. And the thing nobody said in a yard in 188 is said by him, in a room, in his own mouth: a remedy that undoes the scheme and does not undo the price produces a district where nobody can get a cart (199:67).**

### The two things that were answered, and they were the loudest threads

- **THE LEASE. The discretion ran out, nobody exercised it, nobody refused it, and the account has not moved on it in a month — and the reason is given in the account's own mouth by the man who is the office: to take a granary off a district the account has to put a person in the district, and there is no clerk on the register and no form for it and there has not been one since before he came into the post, and he has been asked twice in nine years and said no twice and was right twice.** And a reeve of fifty-four has been holding a granary and a bank of the second mouth for about a month on a word of her own and a door that shuts at the seventh bell, **and that is not a right, there is no line for it, and he could not produce it in a room if she asked him to** (199:33, 199:35, 199:39). She asks for one thing: that it go in a book written by somebody who is not her. **The granary is not renewed and it is not lost.**
- **THE HARVEST IS IN, AND THE DISTRICT EATS, AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE RUINED — and the person who arranged it did not arrange it for us.** About eleven thousand loads and about seven thousand, about three thousand four hundred in the cut, about three thousand one hundred of them on carts belonging to somebody else, a hundred loads short at fifteen pounds, a hundred and forty loads into a book for eighteen pounds thirteen shillings and fourpence with no claim on it and nothing asked for, and a lease about a hundred and seventy-five loads that nobody has renewed and nobody has refused and nobody has been told is there (196:19–27). **And the one figure the reeve has never put on her own wall in nine years is the number of a lease, and she says so, and nobody writes it (196:37, 196:39).** It is in because about forty people in a district of six hundred did ordinary work in the ordinary way for nine weeks and a day, and the only thing he did was the thing the whole volume has been about, which is to write down what each of them said in their own words and keep the three of them apart — the district's own account, the account's own book, and the register (196:47, 196:49).

### The new question, which is on the page for the first time and is not answered

**Whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment.** It is on the page in Chapter 198 and it is in a book, and it is not a sentence anybody says. The account's own page for Ryefold has seven lines and the seventh says that the burden stated against the party is entered against **no. 63 of one hundred, which is a number upon a sheet and not a name**; that the person who took it is not a party, is not of the district, **and could not be entered by name, there being no line in that book for a person who is not a party**; that the burden is a year of one man's memory and it is paid; and that **no line in that book says which year, and there is no column for one, and the person who paid it is the only person who could put one there** (198:35).

**And the number is on the outside of a bundle that a reeve put there herself, off one of a hundred numbered sheets, and that sheet is one of ninety-one in a sacking bag with a bit of string on it in a yard nine feet deep between the granary wall and a cart-shed wall, where it has been for a week, and about four hundred carts in about nine hundred yards have had three weeks in which to pick it up and not one of them has been asked (198:7, 198:73). And the person who would know which year it was is standing in that lane, and nobody asked him, and nobody in that lane has ever asked him a question they did not already have the answer to (198:75, 198:77). The white nothing did not put anything up, and it said nothing whatever about a year then and it says nothing whatever about a year now, and it is a thing that records and not a thing that warns (198:79).**

**Nobody in Volume 04 answers it. The System does not answer it and did not raise it. It is not compressed into one word, and 177:91's separation of the year from the woman of twenty-eight in a city four miles away holds: she is in the room at 194 and at 197 and she is not the person who named the price, and he says so out loud in the room before he pays (197:53).**

### What it cost, and it is a relationship and a body and neither is repaired

**He is in a room as a person and not as hands, and there is a form of it nobody asked for: a basket is the only thing in that lane that puts his name in a place where anybody can find it (200:15). And on the last morning of the volume a carter of about thirty asks the reeve out of nothing why a man who cannot read has a line on her wall, and she answers him, and the man is four feet away and hears the whole of it, and not one person in that lane has said a word about it since (200:13, 200:15, 200:17, 200:47). That is the volume's turn and it is not a resolution.**

**Osa Trin is a person in the matter and is one of the four witnesses and is not the carrier, and the chapter says in words that nobody in the room remarks on it, because a witness is not a party and the only difference between a person in a matter and a witness in a matter is which of the two of them is holding the pen (197:43).** He did not ask her in this batch and did not try to. **A wheelwright of fifty-seven came four miles to a room four miles off on a day of his own, with a wheel in his shop, and is not going to be paid for that day either, and says so in about nine words in a corridor, and neither of the two days is repaired and neither is written off (197:90, 197:96).** Sera Quill appears in one chapter, is expressly not his advocate, has not raised her family claim and he has not asked her about it, and **the fourth reason she gave him and the reason he could not ask Osa Trin anything are named as the same shape and are kept apart in two mouths, and one of them says that if anybody ever writes the two of them down as one thing then the record is wrong, and the record being right is the whole of what we have been doing in this room for a year (194:69, 194:71, 194:73).**

### The permanent liability, which grew by two pages and changed shape twice

Three names in a bundle with his own at the foot of the third sheet, four hundred miles away in an office that is not locked. **This batch added the seventeenth and eighteenth pages of a penny exercise book — 197:106 and 200:53 — and the seventeenth says in its own words *I can say all of that. I cannot say which year it was*, which is the first time in two hundred chapters that the permanent cost has been taken and the first time in two hundred chapters that the writing says what is left.**

### The devices, which moved once and did not advance

**The rendering comes up ONCE, at 197:64, in a room, while he is reading the panel, and he is in a bad way and nobody noticed — and all four of the outline's conditions hold, and it is the fifth appearance in the volume and it has carried no month-date. ** **The leg has not been his own since the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass and no chapter in this batch states a count of days since it cleared and the word Thornglass appears nowhere in Chapters 191–200**; it is in strong form at 197:66, 200:21 and 200:51, and **it does not clear when the year goes, and 197:66 says in words why: a year is not a thing anybody is carrying, and the only thing that has ever cleared that leg was a piece of paper somebody else was going to have to read, and there was nothing in that room he was carrying for anybody.** **It does not clear anywhere in this batch, and at 200:21 he says he expected it to and told nobody.** The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture and the token are exactly where Volume 03 left them and none of the four is mentioned. **Eight people in the world know he cannot read and the count did not go up and is not restated in any of the ten chapters**; fifteen people in this batch handle a document he cannot read, or a page read back to him, or a board with his name on it, and not one of them derives anything new from it.

### What Volume 02 and Volume 03 left alone and this batch did not advance

**The historical right of refusal. The authorship question and the Shale Mirror. The C—2 chain. The plate. The fifth mark. The preservation authority's custody question. The removed Quiet Chamber page and Sera's duplicate. The Bridgeward, Wen Lask and the toll. The bill and the spring ranking. The page with no successor. The continuity line.** All of them are exactly where Volume 03 left them, **and no chapter in this batch says the word *exception* or prints a form with a word at the top of it, and the continuity line is not on the Shelf's table in Ryefold and is not refused an eleventh time here.** The three sheets on the coast are in an office two hundred miles away and no chapter has produced them and **the word *Saltglass* does not appear in Chapters 191–200.** **Neris Ro and Tamsin Ro are six miles from Ryefold and are not in a scene, Tamsin is not to be called, Bram Orren does not appear, and Ansa Rell does not appear and is not redeemed.** The eleven yards of Volume 03 is not mentioned. **NK-3317 is not explained, not referred to and not asked about, and the volume's new question is not on the page until Chapter 198 and is not answered.** **Nobody is relieved of the sentence he said at 156:63, and no chapter in this batch restates it or names the fourth sitting, the set-aside or the category. The four words are quoted in exactly one place, at 194:43, in the mouth of the clerk who entered them, twenty-nine chapters after they were said, in answer to the question 165:61 planted, and it is recorded in `state/continuity.md` as a decision: the man who entered it thought the man who said it was right, which makes the harm worse and relieves nothing, and the words are said once and are not explained.**

Chapter 200's successor has not been written, a Volume 05 outline has not been planned, and `reviews/` has not been touched.**

Last completed phase: **Volume 04 Batch 0005, Chapters 191–200.** Ten chapters, **one** System panel and it is the volume's fifth and last, **six block-quoted runs and six distinct documents**, **no weekday** on a plural-safe scan, **no Shelf month-date** in the batch, **no narrator intrusion**, **two bold lines in the whole batch and both of them the panel**, no duplicate paragraph over ninety characters in prose, a normalised duplicate scan at seventy characters returning zero, no new enemy, no monster, no fight, no rescue, and nothing won. **The year of his own memory was paid in full, once, in Chapter 197, and he cannot say which year it was. The scheme was exposed in Chapter 199 by the man who owns it, who gave up a register and said about nine thousand out loud himself and was not redeemed. The new question is on the page in Chapter 198 and is not answered and is not to be answered by anybody in this volume.**

## VOLUME 04 IS OPEN — Batch 0004, Chapters 181–190, *The Account*

> **THE SIX THINGS TO READ BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IN THIS SECTION, and five of the six are findings against records and not against chapters.**

Twelve of the sixteen are repaired in place and four are carried on purpose. What you must know: `189:29` said **NINEN** for a boy of nineteen, inside an entry the chapter certifies as `CORRECT`; **the bench's own book misdated three of its own entries and in all three the chapters were right and the book is what moved**; `185:89` called the forty-eighth week *the first week after the cut* and it is the second, which **leaves a live contradiction with 173:55 in Batch 0003 that is carried and not repaired and is standing defect 21**; `186:3` and `189:27` said four hundred idle carts had come round for the first time in seven *weeks*, and 182:5 has said seven **years**; `190:3` and `190:9` disagreed about the bell inside one chapter; and **the hundred numbered sheets did not add up — ninety-one were in Brack's sacking bag and in Sena Ollet's basket at once, and 190:99 already contradicted 190:79 inside its own chapter.** The full itemisation, with the working and the instrument, is in `state/continuity.md` under FINDING 11.**

It does not: the woman with four children and a stove and no paper is NESH ARRAD, thirty-nine, named at 168:67 and 171:100, and the second day of the forty-seventh week is Chapter 180, and 180:39 gives *you go six miles, you pay a shilling and fourpence, you write one name on one sheet in a second hand* and 180:47 gives the office's own nine months. It is FOUR HUNDRED AND FIFTY times, exactly — £60 is 14,400 pence and 14,400 ÷ 32 = 450. Forty-five is £6 over 32d. It is corrected in place to 172:67. That is the fifth time in this project a state file has cited a line that does not exist and the first time one has been found by a check as cheap as asking.** The habit is not *be careful*. It is: print the line, and look at it.

### What this batch is

**A piece of paper in this county is priced at what it is worth to a man who already has a cart, and that is a fact about how a register is written and not a claim about anybody, and the man who wrote the register was in a yard for three hours and was right about every word he said.** The batch's spine is **the difference between what a document costs and what it is valued at, and the finding is that the two are the same number, so the load is not in it.** It is stated in its plainest form at 185:57, in a room four miles off Ryefold, in the mouth of a boy of nineteen who is nineteen and is not sorry: the register values a sheet at what it saves, and what it saves is a load, and a load in this district's own book is two shillings and eightpence, and a sheet costs two shillings and eightpence, **so the register's own valuation of a piece of paper, translated into this district's own book, comes to exactly the price of the piece of paper, and the load is not in it.**

**There is no blight, no famine, no fight, no rescue, no monster, no new enemy and no romance beat. Nothing was won. Nobody was relieved of anything and nobody was redeemed and nothing was undone and the lease is not renewed and the granary is not lost.**

### The four things that happened

- **A discretion is not a refusal, and a thing which has run out has not been withdrawn.** A reeve of fifty-four, twenty-two years in that granary, has never had a discretion in writing, has never known how long one ran, has never been refused anything and has never been given anything, and the whole of the protection a granary of six hundred people has got is one door that shuts at the seventh bell and her own hand on it. **And the lease has got about a hundred loads cheaper — fifteen pounds — because the district's own cut emptied the granary on carts the district does not own, and both figures are "about" and the convenor makes her write the word twice.** The whole of the lease sheet is block-quoted for the first time in the volume and its fourth line points at a building six miles up a road that goes north and says the terms may be had on application, and there is no form for asking. 181.
- **The practice was done to the man who invented it.** A carter of forty-six offers in a lane, outside the second door, in front of about nine people and the carts, without being asked, and Ilyan is not allowed to ask him; the asking happens four days later in a room, in the same nine words, and the man says yes again and says both things have to go in the book in that order. **The reader of the district has already declined, unasked, in a doorway, on the grounds that a district of six hundred people that can only be a person in a room one way is one woman and a slate.** 181, 184.
- **A form can be numbered, and it cuts both ways, and the same boy of nineteen says both halves out loud.** A hundred numbered sheets at one pound and five shillings, paid for by a man of thirty-four out of his own with the price said before the fact. **A name has to be spoken by somebody who has met the man; a number can be written into a book in a hand that has never seen him, in a building he has never been in, in a year when he is not thinking about it, and a clerk who has never met him can read it out in a room. And the same fact cuts the other way and he gives that half for nothing: a number can be produced against the man, and there is nobody in that room who can stand up and say it is not him, because you cannot say that about a number.** 182, 187, and the volume's fourth and last panel at 187:39–40.
- **He is right about it and it is still a harm, and the two facts are not in tension and the room cannot make them one.** A Chancellor of fifty-four in a yard for three hours, about seven thousand loads, four true things and a fifth that is his, and the chapter's statement of the finding is that they are **two different questions and only one of them has a person in it: *is the price right* has a man in it and he is in the yard and he answers it, and *who is the price right for* has nobody in it, because the answer is not a sentence — it is a column, and there is no column.** And the next thing, which nobody said: a remedy that undoes the scheme and does not undo the price produces a district where nobody can get a cart, and the man who said it first in a yard in the forty-third week is not in that yard. 188.

### The four things that did not happen, which are this batch's real subject

- **The year is not taken.** 185:123 and 185:125: the first of the forty-eighth week came, and nothing was taken, and nothing was paid, and the matter has been granted a day in the next half of the year so it is carried for a year. 190:97: *that is not because I did anything.* **A stage is not paid for twice and the payment is at 197 or 198.**
- **The lease is not renewed and is not lost.** The discretion has run out, nobody has exercised it, nobody has refused it, nothing has been taken. **The account's answer to its own run-out discretion was to send the one man in that building who cannot be sent anywhere else, because there is no clerk on the register and no form for asking, and he came for three hours about a granary and went back up the road.** 181, 188.
- **The sheets are not taken away and are not condemned.** 188:57 is the Chancellor's answer — *a remedy that takes the price away and leaves the sheets is not a remedy, it is a change of weather* — and 188:77 is the thing nobody in that yard said. **Nothing is undone, nothing is condemned, and Halm's first scheme is not exposed here: that is Chapter 199 and his network survives and he gives up the register and not the other nine thousand.**
- **The new question is not asked.** *Whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment* is not on the page in any of Chapters 181–190. **177:91 separates the year from the woman of twenty-eight in a city four miles away in the same sentence, and neither her name nor her age appears in any of the ten chapters and they are not joined.**

- **The two hundred and forty-first name is on a list six miles up the road that goes north and it is not shut, and she is not mentioned in any of the ten chapters by name.** Nesh Arrad, thirty-nine, four children, a stove and no paper, put at the bottom of a list of two hundred and forty names in a reeve's own hand in a different ink with no authority anywhere in this county for a reeve to do it (168:67), entered on the account's own list in a second hand against a form at a shilling and fourpence in Chapter 180 (180:39, 180:45), with the office's own nine months said at 180:47 and nobody in the district to hear anything about it until then. **190:95 restsates her without naming her, which is this volume's own rule about names. The thread is now in this file and in the Batch 0005 prompt, and it was in neither until the review pass of 26 September 2026 — and the review that noticed 190:95 was looking for an orphan and found a woman who has been on the page since Chapter 168.**

### What it cost him, which is a relationship and not a body and is not repaired

**He could not ask her, and this time she spoke first.** Osa Trin is a person in the matter and he is carrying a matter and he is not allowed to ask her anything. **In Chapter 181 she comes down the lane unasked and says why she is not going to sign, and the reason she gives is the batch's best argument against him: if the only people who can sign anything in this district are the people who can read anything in this district, then this district is one woman and a slate.** And in 185:109, in a corridor, she tells him the sentence in the minute is a thought he had three weeks ago in a doorway, and says **that is not a thing you did and I want it said that way round.** That is the same shape as the fourth reason a woman of twenty-eight gave him in a city four miles from that room, and he paid it once and did not get an argument, and the two are kept apart. **And the second thing he cannot do cost him nothing to say and bought nothing: the three hundred and thirty-nine are the one class in this county with an instrument pointed at it, the instrument is a boy of nineteen, there is one of him, and he cannot ask him for a second thing while this one is open, and he is not going to ask him.** 186:83.

### The permanent liability, which grew by two pages and changed shape once

Three names in a bundle with his own at the foot of the third sheet, four hundred miles away in an office that is not locked. **This batch added the fifteenth and sixteenth pages of a penny exercise book — 185:127 and 190:93 — and a number on a sheet in a reeve's hand six miles up a road that goes north, which is the first time in this volume that the district has put a NUMBER into a book about itself rather than a name, and it is the shape the whole batch was for: a number is the cheapest thing in the world to put on a person and the most useful thing in the world to put on a document, and it is the same piece of paper and the same hand, and whoever is holding the pen decides which of the two it turns out to be.** 190:65.

### The devices, which did not move, and one of them did not appear

**The rendering DOES NOT APPEAR in any of Chapters 181–190 and is not referred to.** It has come up four times in Volume 04 by the end of this batch, at 151:51 on a river bank, at 160:78 in a yard, at 166:117 in a yard and at 177:11 in a cart, at the same height and the same distance each time, and the passes in this volume have been over those lines and none of them needed another appearance, and a further appearance in a volume starts to be a tic rather than a device. **THE LEDGER IS CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0005 REVIEW PASS: the list omitted 151:51, which four passes had missed, and carried the Chapter 166 one at 166:111, which is a different line and is *The fee has been paid.* **The leg has not been his own since the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass and no chapter in this batch states a count of days since it cleared and the word Thornglass appears nowhere in the ten chapters**; it is in strong form three times, at 183:5, 183:94, 185:121 and 186:87 — **183:54 was cited for the leg in the first writing of this record and 183:54 is a factor on the fourpence difference** — and it goes once, unremarked, at 190:87 and 190:89, for about four hours in a cart shed, and the chapter says in words that there is no part of this in which anybody found out anything. **Eight people in the world know he cannot read and the count did not go up and is not restated in any of the ten chapters, and eleven people in this batch handle a document he cannot read and not one of them derives anything new from it.** The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture and the token are exactly where Volume 03 left them and none of the four is mentioned in this batch.

### What Volume 02 and Volume 03 left alone and this batch did not advance

**The historical right of refusal. The authorship question and the Shale Mirror. The C—2 chain. The plate. The fifth mark in the guild's count book. The preservation authority's custody question. The removed Quiet Chamber page and Sera's duplicate. The Bridgeward, Wen Lask and the toll. The bill and the spring ranking. The page with no successor. The continuity line.** All of them are exactly where Volume 03 left them. **The only thing this batch says about a form with a word at the top of it is its opposite, and it says so in a printer's shed and not in a room: there is no word at the top of it, and there is a number, and there is nowhere on it for a name, and the last line on its face is the printer's own and it says nothing is to be written on it by the person who takes it (182:87, 182:91).** The three sheets on the coast are in a bin in an office two hundred miles away and no chapter has produced them and **the word *Saltglass* does not appear in Chapters 181–190.** **Neris Ro and Tamsin Ro are six miles from Ryefold and are not in a scene and Tamsin is not to be called. Sera Quill does not appear and her family claim stays unfiled and he may not speak for it.**

## VOLUME 04 IS OPEN — Batch 0003, Chapters 171–180, *Carrying It*

> **THE ONE THING TO READ BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IN THIS SECTION, and it is about arithmetic rather than about a chapter.** `outline/volume-04.md` gives the working for the difference between a year of protection and the carts it was spent on as *462 − 460 = £2 3d*. **£462 − £460 is £2 exactly, and no chapter of Volume 04 stated the difference until 169:17, which states it correctly.** **Chapter 169 is not to be "corrected" by any pass and this batch did not touch it.** The full finding, with the mechanism, is in `state/continuity.md` under **FINDING 1**, in the Batch 0002 section below.
>
> **AND THE SECOND FINDING, WHICH IS A RECORD ERROR AND NOT A PROSE ERROR.** Three state files quoted a sentence as Sena Ollet's and as the volume's thesis — *Nobody in this district is being cheated. Nobody in this district is being told anything* — and cited Chapters 151:77 and 151:85. **A full-text scan of all one hundred and eighty chapter files returns no instance of it. It was not installed in a chapter and Batch 0003 did not install it and does not quote it.** What is on the page instead is at 169:59 and 169:61, in the mouth of a woman of about sixty-one whose name nobody in that room will ever find out, and no chapter in Volume 04 names her. See **FINDING 2**.
>
> **AND THREE THINGS THIS BATCH'S OWN PASS FOUND IN THE FILES IT WAS INHERITING, which are Findings 5, 6 and 7 and are all in `state/continuity.md` with the working.** **Finding 5: the Batch 0003 prompt's clock table put Chapter 171 on Shelf day forty-one, which cannot be the first day of the cut, and expected a day-count instance at fifty-one, which is not a day this batch reaches; Chapter 171 is day forty-two and the two new day-counts are forty-eight and fifty.** **Finding 6: Chapter 167:41's hundred printed forms at *about two pounds five* is wrong, because a hundred at threepence is three hundred pence, which is one pound and five shillings, and two state files asserted the wrong sum and printed the right check on the same line.** **Finding 7: three state files gave Ormond Ketch as saying he would have come on the thirty-first day of the forty-sixth week, which is out of band, against 163:57's sixth day of that week, which is day forty-seven; all three are corrected.**
>
> **The evidence warning is unchanged.** `.gitignore` contains `logs/` and `*.log`, no cited review log exists, and no claim that a log file exists should be believed without a file that can be opened. **Every count in this batch's record carries its line, and one hundred and twenty-nine distinct chapter-and-line citations were written into `state/continuity.md` in this pass and every one of them was then printed and checked, and twenty of the first hundred and four were wrong and are corrected.** The habit is not *be careful*. It is: print the line, and look at it.

### What this batch is

**A man of thirty-one who cannot read carried a piece of paper for seven days and found out that carrying it is the thing that costs, and that the price of it is a year of his own memory, and he said the price out loud in a room with eleven people in it and nobody in the room can check it.** The batch's spine is **the difference between the length of a harm and the instrument that fixes it**, and it is stated in its plainest form at 180:39 in the mouth of a woman of thirty-nine walking six miles up a road: a harm that is one person long is a day, a shilling and fourpence and a second hand; a harm that is four hundred people long is a page; **and a page cannot undo anything.**

**There is no blight, no famine, no fight, no rescue, no monster, no new enemy and no romance beat. Nothing was won. Nobody was relieved of anything and nobody was redeemed and nothing was undone.**

### The four things that happened

- **The promise was kept and it was worth nine people.** Osa Trin read two hundred and forty names out loud in a weighhouse with the door open on the first day of the cut, at the first bell, in nine minutes of reading and forty-two of waiting, and he stood in the one door of that building and read nothing at all. **About forty people watched a man who cannot read stand in a door and about nine of them worked out why, and one of the nine was the woman reading the list, and she had asked him not to say and did not say.** The fourth line was read out and the household of a man who died in the thirty-first week of last year was standing in that building and does not know. **And nothing happened, and the chapter says nothing happened, because a public reading of a list is not a hearing and a man in a door cannot make it into one.** 171.
- **A hundred loads came off a quay in a day and three quarters and the price of a day was said out loud in a yard.** A carter of thirty-four worked out that a hundred loads is a quarter of one cart, and that a quarter of seven days is a day and three quarters, and that fifty-seven loads a day at the register's own three shillings is eight pounds eleven a day, **and that the only price of a day anybody in that district writes down is ninepence, in chalk on a wall, and that the two figures are two hundred and twenty-eight to one.** He did not want to give the day and he gave it and asked for his name to be on a list, and the list is one name long. 172.
- **A matter was opened that has to be held open past its review, and he could not open it.** A bench of four was asked whether it would be a party. **Gant said no, for the right reason, and the reason is that he signed an application he could not read on the twenty-fourth day of the forty-third week and a district of six hundred people lost a year on the word *decided*. Tamworth said yes for the wrong reason, which was that he has asked four times for something to do that mattered. Hake found the answer nobody had said: a bench is not a party, four people is four persons, one of them makes it and one of them stands in the room and the other three are witnesses.** And Gant said a name out loud, and a date, and ten shillings and eightpence, and it was not a ruling. 173, 174, 175.
- **Stage 4, Burden Bearer, arrived, late, by a practice, a failure, a relationship and a permanent cost, and the cost was named and not paid.** Three people were asked out loud in a lane in nine words whether they would like to be the one standing there. Two said yes. **One said no in four seconds, and the no was correct, and he did not persuade and did not come back with it in different words.** A wheelwright said yes and cannot be paid and will not be promised to, and that is the failure and it cost a person. Osa Trin was not asked, because she is a person in the matter. **And the year was named: one year of his own out of thirty-one, the last of the years in which he was in a room with a person he had not yet met, and it is not a fog and not a wound and not a gift and not a curse, and nobody has taken it, and the reason it is the last one is that the not was available and he took it.** 177.

### The four things that did not happen, which are this batch's real subject

- **The year is not taken.** It is named at 177:87 and 177:91 in front of eleven people and it is not paid at 177:95, and the payment is at 197 or 198. **A stage is not paid for twice.**
- **The lease is not dealt with and it is worse than before.** The discretion ran to the cut, the cut ended at the ninth bell on the forty-eighth day, and on the fiftieth there is nothing on the nail about it. **A district of six hundred people is holding a granary and a bank of the second mouth on a discretion that has run out, and the nail has three things on it and it is a nail.** 180:7, 180:67.
- **The carrying is not taken off.** Ryefold is still a party uncooperative on the account's own record in a yard six miles up a road north, and no form exists for a person named in a deposition to ask for a thing to be out, and a forbearance may be applied for once a year and this was the year.
- **The new question is not asked.** *Whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment* is not on the page in any of Chapters 171–180. **177:91 names the year and separates it in the same sentence from the woman of twenty-eight in a city four miles away, precisely so that the two are not joined, and they must not be joined.**

### What it cost him, which is a relationship and not a body and is not repaired

**He could not ask her.** Osa Trin is a person in the matter and he is carrying a matter and asking her to carry it would be arranging her into a record, which is the thing he said out loud in a yard on the twenty-fifth day of the forty-third week that he would not do. **So the practice went to a carter and a wheelwright and the reader of his district was not asked, and the one good thing that happened in the batch happened without him and he had no part in it.** That is the same shape as the fourth reason a woman of twenty-eight gave him in a room in a city four miles from that room, and he paid it once and did not get an argument. And the day he gave away on a towpath trying to do it alone was one of nine he had, and the forms are in a box that is not in this district, and the man with a key is six miles off and cannot be reached in a day, and that is the sentence Chapter 167 ended on and it has not improved in thirteen days.

### The permanent liability, which grew by two pages and changed shape once

Three names in a bundle with his own at the foot of the third sheet, four hundred miles away in an office that is not locked. **This batch added the thirteenth and fourteenth pages of a penny exercise book — 177:106 and 180:63 — and a form at a shilling and fourpence that he did NOT fill in and did NOT buy, which is the first time in this volume that the fee for being a person in a record was the price of not being one.**

### The devices, which did not move

**The rendering came up once, at 177:11, in a cart, at the same height and the same distance, and did not move and was not explained, and the leg went at the same moment and a carter said nothing about it.** The leg has not been his own since the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass and **no chapter in this batch states a count of days since it cleared**; it is in strong form four times, at 171:34, 174:97, 177:11 and 180:63. **Eight people in the world know he cannot read and the count did not go up.** The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture and the token are exactly where Volume 03 left them and none of the four is mentioned in this batch.

### What Volume 02 and Volume 03 left alone and this batch did not advance

**The historical right of refusal. The authorship question and the Shale Mirror. The C—2 chain. The plate. The fifth mark in the guild's count book. The preservation authority's custody question. The removed Quiet Chamber page and Sera's duplicate. The Bridgeward, Wen Lask and the toll. The bill and the spring ranking. The page with no successor. The continuity line.** All of them are exactly where Volume 03 left them. **The only thing this batch says about a form with a word at the top of it is a man describing handing one to a carter at 175:61, and the word *exception* is not used as a device anywhere in the batch.** The three sheets on the coast are in a bin in an office two hundred miles away and no chapter has produced them. **Neris Ro and Tamsin Ro are six miles from Ryefold and are not in a scene and Tamsin is not to be called.** Bram Orren, Ansa Rell, Sel Harrow, Corva Silt, Wenna Tace, Meridith Vale and Dessin Nahl do not appear. **Ors Halm is not named in this batch at all**; he is referred to twice without being named, at 174:73 and 180:57, and the only occasion of the volume's own use of him is the nine months, and 180:57 says it is a different nine months. **Sera Quill does not appear and her family claim stays unfiled and he may not speak for it.**

## VOLUME 04 IS OPEN — Batch 0002, Chapters 161–170, *The Category, and the Words That Made It*

> **THE ONE THING TO READ BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IN THIS SECTION, and it is about arithmetic rather than about a chapter.** `outline/volume-04.md` gives the working for the difference between a year of protection and the carts it was spent on as *462 − 460 = £2 3d*. **£462 − £460 is £2 exactly, and no chapter of Volume 04 had ever stated the difference** — four chapters gave both figures and none subtracted them, which is why a wrong figure survived in the outline and in the state files for a whole batch without anybody being able to find it by reading prose. **Chapter 169 states the difference for the first time in the manuscript and states it correctly, as two pounds. A later pass must not "correct" Chapter 169, which is right.** The full finding, with the mechanism and the reason this class of error can only be caught by a script, is in `state/continuity.md` under **FINDING 1**.
>
> **AND THE SECOND FINDING, WHICH IS A RECORD ERROR AND NOT A PROSE ERROR.** Three state files quoted a sentence as Sena Ollet's and as the volume's thesis — *Nobody in this district is being cheated. Nobody in this district is being told anything* — and cited Chapters 151:77 and 151:85 for it. **Two of the three files had it and a full-text scan of all one hundred and seventy chapters returns no instance of it. It has been corrected in place in both files and it was NOT installed in a chapter, because writing canon to make a record true is the same class of act as swapping a number.** The sentence that is on the page instead is at 169:59, and the sentence itself at 169:61, in the mouth of a woman of about sixty-one who came down to a quay for grain and is not on a list and whose name nobody in that room will ever find out. See **FINDING 2**.
>
> **The evidence warning is unchanged and it applies to this batch's own record.** `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and three other cited review logs do not exist, have never existed, and cannot be committed: `.gitignore` contains `logs/` and `*.log`. **No claim that a log file exists should be believed without a file that can be opened, and no count in any of these files should be believed without the line number beside it.** Every count in this batch's record carries its line, and the standing instruction for Volume 04 is unchanged: **re-run the grep against the chapter files, and do not re-read the list.**

### What this batch is

**A man of thirty-one who cannot read said a true sentence at a counter, about a category, in the abstract, and about himself, and it has cost a district of six hundred people a year, and he is not going to be excused for it.** The batch's spine is **a number that is in one place and a burden that is in another, and a woman who carries the number and is not told what it is** — which was Batch 0001's spine, unchanged and paid off. The engine is the same engine: a temporary exception is a permission, a permission is exercised by anybody the holder points at it, and nothing in the Bench's form asks who exercises it.

**There is no blight, no famine, no fight, no rescue, no monster, no new enemy, and no romance beat. Nothing was won.**

### The four things that happened

- **The reversal, and it is the series outline's own line.** Ryefold's claim for a fresh forbearance is **set aside — not refused — at the fourth sitting**, on the ground that the district has been carried as uncooperative. **The sentence that does it quotes a deposition of a man who was not in the room, in a hand that is not his, out of a conversation he had with a clerk about a category in the abstract, in which he said that a district that sends nobody has decided it has nothing to say.** The words are his, the fifth box of the deposition is true, the chair says *none of it is disputed*, and the harm is done by a weekly posting and by the word *decided*. **The wording is *that* and not *which*, and this file said *which* for a whole batch, which is the minute's substitution and the single word the chapter turns on: *that* at 156:63, 165:55 and 165:121, *which* at 165:125, and the swap counted at 165:127.** **He is in a corridor and is not in the room, and no form exists anywhere in that county for a man to put himself in a room where a thing about his own district is being decided, and that absence has a name nobody has needed.**
- **A boy of nineteen who is kind and who asked exactly one question.** Amos Kellock, two years in the account's own office, entitled to take depositions for eleven months, the first person anybody in that building has met who reads for pleasure. **The form has six boxes and the boxes are not numbered, and the first five are the same in every matter and the sixth is whatever the matter turns on; on this one it turned on the man in it, and the box wants the man himself to say it, and the man was in a cart shed four miles off and the chamber and the account do not speak, so on the morning of the sitting he walked the six miles before the second bell and asked the only question that box asks — *Whose words were they, and of what* — and he got the true answer, and he wrote the true answer down, and he says in a yard that he is not sorry because he had a box and a true answer and no second box on the form and he has looked for one (166:83–93, 165:57, 165:71).** He is nineteen, he is not a villain, he is doing a job, and the room did exactly what his being right deserved. **Two earlier passes got this wrong in opposite directions and both errors are recorded: the first said he asked nothing and had not used ten seconds, which was false of him and incompatible with a form that records the deponent's own statement about the abstract; the second said the form has a box asking *whether any district is named in it*, which is not on it. The form as printed in 165:45–59 and 164:59–71 is the only authority on what is on it.**
- **A public answer, made by a person, in the open, in numbers, in a district where one has never been made.** A granary reeve answers questions from her own quay for half an hour — eleven thousand loads, three thousand four hundred in the cut, four carts, three thousand one hundred on hire, three thousand paid for, a hundred loads short, fifteen pounds, an account of nine thousand seven hundred pounds charging four hundred and eighty-five a year, a notice at five shillings and fourpence, a lease at a fortieth of everything in the granary, a ration list of two hundred and forty names with a dead man in the fourth line, a reeve's wage eleven weeks out of two pounds ten a year and ten shillings and sevenpence. **About two hundred and forty people hear it. Nobody has any idea what to do with it. She says she is not going to do it again for at least a month.**
- **A quantity of released capacity that is a hundred and seventy times what is needed, in the wrong county, two days too late.** Thirty-four men who bought a sheet and do not want it have each unbooked a second cart. A second cart is five hundred loads. Thirty-four of them is seventeen thousand loads. **The district is a hundred loads short, and a hundred loads is fifteen pounds, and the district has nothing, and the carts are eleven miles off, and a loaded cart cannot go on a towpath in the afternoon.** A factor works all of it out in a room on the back of a form, and a bench of four writes thirty-four names in a book, and the batch's finding is that a page is the only instrument anybody in that room has for a sum of four hundred private things, and a page cannot go to Nethergill on one day and be a person in a lane on the next, and a page is not a person.

### The four things that did not happen, which are this batch's real subject

- **Nobody was relieved of the sentence.** He is not excused, not corrected, not contextualised, and nobody says *what he meant was*. **A man relieved of the sentence he said is not corrected, he is excused, and the whole of this volume's arc is the difference.** In the yard where he is told, he does four things nobody would forgive him for not doing, and the chapter says so at 166:57 and it is the only place in ten chapters where the narrator states what a man did not do.
- **Stage 4, Burden Bearer, is not arrived.** He is Stage 3 and is Stage 3 at the end of Chapter 170, and none of the seven devices has moved. Stage 4 arrives in Batch 0003 by a practice, a failure, a relationship and a permanent cost, and its permanent cost — a year of his own memory — is paid at the volume's climax and not when the stage is reached.
- **The lease is not dealt with.** The tenancy ended on Shelf day fourteen, the first day of the forty-second week and the day the notice went up the lane (156:9, 161:3, 161:99), the account holds a discretion that runs to the cut, the renewal is a fortieth of everything in the granary — about two hundred and seventy-five loads, payable in grain, before the cut — and nobody has done anything about it. **Batch 0003 opens one day before the cut.**
- **The new question is not asked.** *Whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment* is not on the page in either Volume 04 batch and may not be. The Volume 03 question is inherited untouched, and a letter that will ask part of it is on its way to a shed four hundred miles away and nobody in this volume will ever know what is in it.

### What it cost him, which is not a body and is not repaired

**A year of a district's protection, caused by a sentence he said about himself and did not mean to be about a district, and a sixth production that nobody performed.** He walked six miles to an office to find out whether he had said a true thing, and there was nothing at the end of the road for him, and he got a form with his own name at the foot of it, and eight days later that form was in a room. **Being produced is a thing done to a man; this was a thing he did to himself in order to find out what he was, and the sentence he said in Chapter 154 about being asked and being produced being different acts is still true and does not apply to him, and he said so himself in a yard.** And he was thirty-one days late about a building with nineteen shelves in it, and gave the reason out loud, and the reason is that he was waiting to be useful. **He was in it on the seventh day of the fortieth week, which is Shelf day six; Chapter 167 had said the forty-first week and twenty-nine days and both were wrong, and the prose is repaired.** And he said yes, before he had worked out what it was, to standing in a doorway on the first day of the cut for no reason that can be written down.

### The permanent liability, which grew by two books and changed shape twice

Three names in a bundle with his own at the foot of the third sheet, four hundred miles away in an office that is not locked. **This batch added a form with his name at the foot of it in a file in a yard six miles up a road north that is also not in a locked room, and a chalk line on a wage board in a district he has been in forty days, and a page in a penny exercise book, and a book in a room above a chandler's shop. The broker's fee is that somebody can send for the broker's own paper, and it has been paid twice in one batch, and the second time it was paid by the boy who wrote it, walking six miles of towpath, and he was thanked by nobody and did not expect to be.**

### The devices, which did not move

**The rendering came up twice in the volume, once in each batch, both times in a yard, at the same height and the same distance, and did not move and was not explained.** **The leg has not been his own since the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass, and no chapter in either Volume 04 batch states a count of days since it cleared, because the journey from the coast to the Shelf was never dated in prose.** It goes seven times in twenty chapters and is his own for about four hours while carrying a basket on a towpath, unremarked, because the finding written down four years ago is that the paper was the weight and the woman was not — **and there was no woman in it at all this time, and he noticed that in Chapter 155 and has still not resolved it.** **About nine people in Ryefold have watched something happen to him and not one of them has ever mentioned it, and the batch says so in the past tense twice.**

### What Volume 02 and Volume 03 left alone and this batch did not advance

**The historical right of refusal. The authorship question and the Shale Mirror. The C—2 chain. The plate. The fifth mark in the guild's count book. The preservation authority's custody question. The removed Quiet Chamber page and Sera's duplicate. The Bridgeward, Wen Lask and the toll. The bill and the spring ranking. The page with no successor. The continuity line.** All of them are exactly where Volume 03 left them. **The only thing this batch says about refusal is a worker's mouth at 167:11 — a sheet with the word *exception* on it takes the paper and not the clause — and a factor's colder version of the same thing at 167:105 — and that is Volume 02 canon arriving in a district as a hope, and it is not the removed clause and no chapter says it is.** The continuity line is not on the Shelf's table in this batch and is not refused an eleventh time here. **The three sheets on the coast are in a bin two hundred miles away and no chapter has produced them.** Neris Ro and Tamsin Ro are six miles from Ryefold and are not in a scene. Bram Orren, Ansa Rell, Sel Harrow, Corva Silt, Wenna Tace, Meridith Vale and Dessin Nahl do not appear. Ors Halm does not appear in this batch and is not softened. **Sera Quill does not appear in this batch and her family claim stays unfiled and he may not speak for it and the reason she gave is the reason and no batch may improve on it.** Nadia Kestrel is not a person in this world and is not a route and is not a rescue.

## VOLUME 04 IS OPEN — Batch 0001, Chapters 151–160, *The Second Mouth and the Counter That Was Always Open*

> **THE ONE THING TO READ BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IN THIS SECTION, and it is not about a chapter.** Chapters 1–150 are canon and Volume 03 is closed. Volume 04's outline did not exist until this phase wrote it, because the Volume 03 close recommended that one be commissioned and did not commission it; the controller's continuation prompt for this phase directed that the next volume be planned and its first batch written, and that is what this phase did. **`outline/volume-04.md` is therefore new and is the first thing an agent should read, and `NOVEL_SPEC.md`'s `## Status` block is wrong about Volume 03 in a way that has been left for its owning phase and must not be believed.**
>
> **THE EVIDENCE WARNING CARRIED FORWARD FROM THE VOLUME 03 CLOSE, and it applies to this batch's own record.** `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and three other cited review logs do not exist, have never existed, and cannot be committed: `.gitignore` contains `logs/` and `*.log`. The findings those passes are said to have returned survive only as prose inside a state file describing its own repairs, which is the exact class of record this project has found wrong six times in a hundred and fifty chapters. **No claim that a log file exists should be believed without a file that can be opened, and no count in any of these files should be believed without the line number beside it.** Every count in this batch's record carries its line, and the standing instruction for Volume 04 is unchanged: **re-run the grep against the chapter files, and do not re-read the list.**

### What this batch is

**A man of thirty-one who cannot read spent twenty-three days on the Ninefold Shelf in a district of six hundred people where every list is a list of weeks and there is no column anywhere for a thing that was not kept — except that on this shelf every list is a list of prices, and the volume's discovery is that this is the same shape and it is a different coin.** The batch's spine is **a number that is in one place and a burden that is in another, and a woman who carries the number and is not told what it is.** The engine is that a temporary exception is a permission, a permission is exercised by anybody the holder points at it, and nothing in the Bench's form asks who exercises it, because the form asks who carries the burden — and the burden is on a district, and the district is not the party who moves the grain.

**There is no blight, no famine, no fight, no rescue, no monster, no new enemy, no romance beat beyond four sentences about a sheet of paper, and nothing that makes the world bigger. Nothing was won.**

### The four things that happened

- **A harvest that is good and is being taken.** A bad year three years back was made permanent by a piece of stationery. The district was granted a year's forbearance on a grain account of **£9,240 at one shilling in the pound, which is £462 a year exactly**; the year was worth **£462** and the district spent **£460** of it hiring carts it did not own, because a hard season's cartage is about three thousand loads at three shillings and the exception was the only money in the district. **The hire went on the account, so the account is now £9,700 and the interest is £485 a year, and the year of protection made the debt two hundred and thirty pounds bigger and the annual charge twenty-three pounds bigger.** Nobody in the district knew any of it. The forbearance expired in the week before the volume opened and the district found out from a reeve who asked a basket-runner, who did not know either.
- **A counter that had been open for four years and that one carter walked into.** The standing authority of preservation holds every instrument printed in this city for four years in a series running by plate, where any person may read any of it at fourpence a sheet. It is canon, it has a man with a bad hand in it, and **no person had ever asked, and the reason is not a locked door — nobody had ever known.** A man of thirty-four walked in because he could not read a slip and was too proud to go back.
- **A warrant with a name on it, and the finding that a burden with a name is a thing that can be sold.** A **prize** is two sheets at a clerk's fee of one shilling and fourpence, it is the same price in a hundred and one counties, and it buys a man with one cart a place at the front of a queue that is the first bad week. **Three hundred and thirty-nine of the three hundred and seventy in this county are held by a person with one cart. A thing worth nine pounds and threepence costs two shillings and eightpence, and the reason is that the man selling it is not selling you the thing.**
- **The System recognised nothing and translated one record, and said one thing that is not decided.** There is one panel in the batch, in the last chapter, two bold lines, and the second is *WHAT IS NOT DECIDED: WHETHER AN EXCEPTION THAT HAS BEEN SOLD IS A THING THAT CAN BE SOLD BACK.*

### The four things that did not happen, which are this batch's real subject

- **The midpoint reversal did not land.** It is planted in Chapter 156 and it lands in **Batch 0002**, in the second half, out of a deposition in his own words. **Nothing in Chapters 151–160 is the reversal and a reader must not be able to read the batch as though it had already happened.**
- **Stage 4, Burden Bearer, is not arrived.** He is Stage 3 and is Stage 3 at the end of Chapter 160, and no device has moved. Stage 4 arrives in Batch 0003 by a practice, a failure, a relationship and a permanent cost, and its permanent cost — a year of his own memory — is paid at the volume's climax and not when the stage is reached.
- **The lease is up on a nail and nothing has been done about it**, and it has four weeks to run, and the remedy nobody in the district can write is planted unspent: **what happens to a man with one cart in a bad week.**
- **The new question is not asked.** *Whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment* is not on the page and may not be. The Volume 03 question — *if the game was a reflection, who made the reflection understand refusal* — is inherited untouched, and a letter that will ask part of it is on its way to a shed four hundred miles away and nobody in this volume will ever know what is in it.

### What it cost him, which is a relationship and not a body

**Sera Quill released the ninth-bridge matter on the public record in order to be free of the fourth term of her own licence, and in the same six minutes refused him the family claim of six years, in a room, with four reasons and none of them a kindness — and she was right, and he agreed with her, and that agreement is the cost.** She asked for her own sheet back, the one with two questions on it in a hand he asked her not to write in, one of which is about consent; **he gave it to her and did not say *you can leave it*, and that is the whole of the first relationship beat in a hundred and sixty chapters.** He was produced nothing and nobody sent for him, and the batch's finding is that being asked and being produced are the same outcome and are not the same act, and the difference is a room with a door in it and one day of a stranger's work, and he has told the coast's story backwards twice now and is not going to be told it a third time.

### The permanent liability, which grew by one book and did not change shape

Three names in a bundle with his name at the foot of the third sheet, four hundred miles away in an office that is not locked, which any clerk there can ask for and copy. **This batch added a fifth book in a district he has never been in, and he put himself in it on purpose, and the only way to be a person in a record instead of a thing in it is to be paid.** His own arithmetic, in his own book: a shed is a penny a night and a wage is ninepence a day, so a year is fifteen pounds four shillings, and he has fifty-seven pence, and the ratio is about sixty to one, and fifty-seven pence is not even two prizes.

### The devices, which did not move

**The rendering came up once, in a yard, with a city in it for the first time in a hundred and sixty chapters, at the same height and the same distance, and did not move and was not explained. The leg has not been his own since the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass, and no chapter in this batch states a count of days since it cleared, because the journey from the coast to the Shelf was never dated in prose. It was his own for about four hours on a towpath while he carried a basket of numbers nobody at either end of it can read, and that is unremarked and is not a discovery. Seven people in the world know he cannot read: two who were told, four who worked it out on a coast four hundred miles behind him and have never said a word, and one who worked it out at a counter in this city about a day and a half ago and told him to go and be four miles away from her for an hour, which he did. He said the number out loud once in the batch, to a bench of four, and it is the first time in his life he has said it.** *(This paragraph is the Batch 0001 record and it is history: **the count was seven after Batch 0001 and it is EIGHT after Batch 0002, and the eighth is Amos Kellock, nineteen, who says it flatly at 164:49 and gets no denial.** See the Batch 0002 section above.)*

### What Volume 02 and Volume 03 left alone and this batch did not advance

**The historical right of refusal. The authorship question and the Shale Mirror. The C—2 chain. The plate. The fifth mark in the guild's count book. The preservation authority's custody question. The removed Quiet Chamber page and Sera's duplicate. The Bridgeward, and Wen Lask, and the toll. The bill and the spring ranking. The page with no successor. The continuity line. The form with a word at the top of it. The fourth term of Sera's licence — released. The family claim of six years — refused.** All of them are exactly where Volume 03 left them. **The only two Volume 02 things this batch touches are the fourth term and the family claim, and it touches the fourth term by ending it and the family claim by refusing it.** Neris Ro and Tamsin Ro are six miles from Ryefold and are not in a scene and may not be put in one. Bram Orren, Ansa Rell, Sel Harrow, Meridith Vale, Wenna Tace and Corva Silt do not appear. Nadia Kestrel is not a person in this world and is not a route and is not a rescue.

### Progress

Current phase: **Volume 04 Batch 0001 complete (151–160).** The next thing is a Volume 04 Batch 0002 prompt, and it was created by this phase at `workspace/volume-04/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` with detailed chapter cards for Chapters 161–170.

Last completed phase: **Volume 04 Batch 0001, Chapters 151–160.** Ten chapters, one new volume outline, ten torn-out pages of a penny exercise book of which the tenth is the first that says anything about a person who is not him, one System panel, four block-quoted documents, and no weekday, no month-date of the Shelf, no new enemy, no monster and no fight.

Last chapter: 0160

Last state record: `state/current.md` — this section. The four Volume 03 batch re-entry guards below it are history and are kept.

**HOW MUCH TO TRUST THIS SECTION.** The chapters in this batch are finished prose and are canon. **The prose defects this batch's own pass found and repaired are in `state/batch-summary.md` under *Verification record* — twelve of them, listed in three parts, of which four were weekday names, three were month-dates, three were a miscounted day in a character's own sum, six were a narrator that knew it was in a book or a page of working-out, and six were bold used as emphasis. The six it found and did NOT repair are in `state/continuity.md` under *Standing defects*, with chapter and line: two are arithmetic in a chapter's own sentence, one is a unit figure in a chapter's own mouth, one is a pair of figures about different things that sit four lines apart and must never be quoted together as one, and two are counts in this batch's own state record that were wrong and are now corrected in four files — the number of people in the world who know he cannot read, which is seven and not three, and the number of times he said so out loud, which is once and not four.** Both of those last two were found by re-reading three chapters and not by re-reading the record, which is the only method in this project that has ever worked. A batch is not complete because the files exist; a later pass should open Chapters 151–160 cold and check the table above against them rather than against this page.
