Do claim that two review passes ran, because they did, and because the reason the second one was needed is the thing a later agent most needs to know: four of the first pass's twenty repairs had not been made, or had been made into something other than what the record claimed, and the record and the commit message both said they had.**

**What this batch delivered, stated once so nothing in it has to be re-read to be known.** The appeal is used twice, and it works both times, and it is a different failure from Volume 02's. **Wend Garrow, thirty-three, stands on four feet of flat stone at the head of the flat on the fourth of the seventh tide at the turn, with about seventy people on it, and asks the one question the stone is for — what is the water going to do — and Bettany Rook answers him out loud, in the open, in about two minutes, without hedging and without asking him for anything: take the cart at the near stones and not in the channel, watch for the water at the far stones going brown at about the quarter past the turn, take the middle of the ebb and not the start of it, you will have the best part of two hours, you will be over in about three quarters of an hour if your beasts are used to a flat.** Nobody asked a second question. Nothing was refused twice. The song was not sung because it is not the seventh. The stone was not treated as evidence. **And the answer was not written down by anybody afterwards as though it were a record, and the man said thank you out loud on the shingle in front of seventy people, and there is nowhere on this coast to put a thing that happened to a man.**

**And then, on the sixth, a man of fifty-four spends the only appeal of his life on a piece of paper in a dealer's hand, and is answered truly, in the open, in front of about seventy people, that there is nothing in that paper that is wrong about the water — which is true, and which is the whole of what was wrong with his week. Nobody refused him. The answer was given in full and it was useless, and the person who gave it is not a bench and not a court and is right about the sea. The batch's finding in one sentence: the road works and the road is two days a week, and the seventh is the one day there is nothing at all.**

**Also delivered, once each. The defence the guild built**: two hundred and sixty yards of stone laid in courses on the seaward side of a nine-foot hole in a gravel bar at the north end, **nineteen years and eight months old, the year the guild had a thing fired in a shed two miles along this coast, and there is not one person on this coast who has ever put the two in the same sentence. The guild was right then and the guild is right now, and the danger is real, and the water has moved, and nobody has looked at it in a long time for a reason that has nothing to do with danger and everything to do with the water moving.** The four things a measurement costs on this coast — **a lead line, a boat, a man who can sound, and a day** — are the same four the glassmaker named at a bench in Chapters 125 and 128, in the same order and the same plain words, and a man on this coast said them out loud without knowing anybody had said them before. **The batch's cost is a day, given away by Cleve Gannock in about nine words in a lane, unasked, with his cart and his pair of beasts and his own afternoon at a landing, and nobody said thank him, and he was two hours late and told the man there it was the beasts.** The measurement is taken and finds **eleven yards of the toe of that wall gone to about two feet of depth, with nothing under the bottom course**, and it is in a book in a shed with a date on it and nobody acted on it. **The batch's milestone: Ilyan asked Bettany Rook, in the open, in front of people, on a good day, whether she would like to be the one standing on the stone, and she said no, and the no is true, and the truth is not the answer he wanted, and he was wrong out loud and she was right out loud, and not one of the nine people who heard it said a word about it then or afterwards, and they end the chapter able to disagree in public and keep working.** The record that is in the world and is useless: **a driven stake in the wall with a guild punch and a number and no person's name, recording a place and not a depth, unlooked at for nineteen years, and the only kind of record on this coast that cannot be produced at all, because it is in the wall.** And the blank wage line, doing on its own what a blank does, in about four hundred words: a clerk two hundred miles off reads a line with a wage on it and no person on it and chooses the option that writes nothing, and the vote of a council of nine sits in a book for a year doing nothing, and nobody asks Seth Garrick, and nobody is going to.

**The batch close: a breach, and a decision, and not a verdict.** On the sixth of the eighth tide, a bar day, with the sea standing on the crown of the bar, at about the seventh bell, the water goes out through the middle of the seaward face of that wall for about eleven yards and makes a boil about the size of a doorway, and six people on a wall see it and **nobody is in it.** The first thing said about it, in the trade's mouth, is *that is the water going out through the middle of it*, and the second is **it is not the wall failing — the wall is doing what it was built to do and it has been doing it for nineteen years — and the third is the finding of the volume so far in a physical object: the harm is not the hole, the harm is the gravel, and the gravel is two feet, and the two feet is the whole of that point, and the first thing the water does on the far side of a breach is take gravel.** It is a shore going, and it will take less than two winters, because this time the guild is putting stone in a hole instead of taking a wall out. And the man who keeps the wall wants said, in the open, by him: **the guild was right.** Then the forward pull, and it is a link and not a monster: **there are three people on this coast who could do something about the eleven yards and not one of them can see the other two, and nobody has asked any of them for anything** — a woman of thirty-six with a boat and a towrope and eleven years of a thing nobody has ever asked her the name of, a woman of fifty-one with a table of tides who has decided not to send a boy up a lane for the number, and a man of sixty-one two miles along the coast whose eighth column is the only figure anybody here has. **And Ilyan's decision, in the dark, at the ninth bell, on a leg that went and did not come back: he is going to go and ask three people whether they would like to be the one standing there. Not to tell them, not to fetch them, not to carry a sheet, not to write one. He has no standing to ask any of them, he has been produced four times already, and his one guess is that at least one of them will say no and that the no will be right.** And there is a fourth road he is not going to go up at all, four miles above the north end, where a woman of eighty-one is knitting and a boy of eight is on a board with a slate, and **she does not know what is under any of it and he has not told her, and nobody on this coast got credit for it. She was told one thing in Chapter 129 — the guild's quarter, the return for her road, and a request that she come to a counter — and she said no to it in about four minutes and did not ask why. That is what Chapter 140's paragraph about her now says; the first draft said she had not been told anything at all, which was false against Chapter 129.**

**What the writer's own pass found and changed before the batch was closed.** **Four calendar slips written into the first draft and all four removed** — *Tuesday* and *Friday* in Chapter 132, and three instances of *Friday* and one of *Wednesday* in Chapter 140. **Four figures that did not close, found in a second sweep over the finished files and all four removed**: a day-count of forty days spoken on the fifth of the seventh tide in Chapter 132, a fortnight where the tide run gives nine days in the same chapter, and a *fourth of the sixth tide* attributed in Chapter 140 to the wrong event. **Three sentences of narration that referred to the book were found and removed**, one of them in a chapter already passed. **The batch card itself carried a figure the tide run does not support — it said the page stopped a cart two days before Chapter 132, and the hold was on the sixth of the sixth tide and Chapter 132 is the fifth of the seventh tide, so the prose says six days, and the same interval is five days in Chapter 131 and seven in Chapter 133, and all three close.** **Two contradictions inside the new geography were found and fixed before the batch was closed**: the wall was first written on the shoulder of the bar, where the ebb on a bar day does not draw the water off it, and it is now on the seaward side of the deep itself, which is where the bar-day physics puts it and which is also what makes the driven mark under nine feet on a good day and standable on a bar day. Straight apostrophes and quotation marks normalised to the house style in all ten chapters.

**What the two cold reviewer passes found afterwards. The first returned twenty-three findings and twenty were repaired in the prose with three recorded as deliberate; the second returned ten more, nine were repaired and one did not hold. Both lists, in full, are in `state/batch-summary.md` — the first in the Verification record, the second under *Third pass*. The nine from the first pass that a later agent must not reintroduce:** **a pane put four hundred miles and two hundred miles off when the guild's two are on the north wall of the tide office a mile up the hill** (the identical defect a repair pass had already fixed in 125, 126, 128 and 130, reintroduced in 136, now corrected, and any distance attached to a pane should be assumed wrong until the chapters are read); **Bettany Rook's career written as six years in the chair plus nineteen before it, which is twenty-five, where the whole of it is nineteen and six of them are the chair**; **the driven mark in three places at once in two chapters, in a wall that is nine courses at the inner end and two at the outer**; **the wall's course arithmetic running on two different course heights in one sentence pair**; **the inference from two hundred and sixty-one against two hundred and sixty-four running backwards, the outer end having come back and not gone out**; **Alma Gannock printed as a man of eighty-one in Chapter 140 one hundred lines after the same chapter called her a woman, and separately asserted not to have been told anything, which is false against Chapter 129**; **the four things attributed to a works' lodging-house kitchen where the glassmaker was not present, and the same misattribution in a woman's mouth, which put a month on the coast's calendar as a time-unit**; and **two sentences of narration that referred to the book or to a narrator, one of them written after the record claimed that none existed.** **And the ten from the second pass, which matter because they are what happens when a record is trusted instead of the chapters:** **the pane's own eighth column had the gravel standing to the fifth course at the outer end of a wall that is two courses tall there, and it is now the inner end**; **Chapter 140's central line had been rewritten by the first pass from *three people knew he went out and not one of them knew what he went out for* into *four people who have all been told by him*, which contradicted the batch's own central unacted-upon fact, named four people no chapter identifies, and was flagged as a canon change nowhere — the original wording is restored**; **the Chapter 138 lift from Chapter 124 had not been rewritten at all, and neither had the chapter's opening, and both are now**; **the tally post's board was in four chapters and not three, with two of them byte-identical across a batch boundary, and both of this batch's instances are now reworded**; **a fifty-five-word run in a wheelwright's mouth, a forty-eight-word run about a slate, two appeal scenes narrated in one sentence, and two narration set-pieces repeated verbatim between chapters, all gone — and a later independent read of the repairs found that Chapter 134 was still carrying about a hundred words lifted from canon Chapter 127, in the painting of the bar staff and the song at the stone, which no reviewer had reported; that is rewritten as well, and the sweep between those two chapters now returns five runs and every one of the five is a fact about the coast or a refrain this batch has already declared**; **the findings count was stated three different ways in three files, one of which left nothing for the three recorded as deliberate, and a byte-identical bullet appeared twice in the batch record — both corrected**; **Chapter 132 had a broken sentence left behind by the first pass's repair; and the one finding that did not hold was a report that Chapter 139 puts the protagonist at thirty, when the man of thirty there has a lamp-oil shed at the head of the lane and is Seth Garrick, and the protagonist is thirty-one in the same chapter and in nine other places.** **The lesson, and it is the reason the second pass existed: the first pass's commit message and its own verification section both asserted that four repairs had been made and two of them had not been made at all. A repair that is described in a record is not a repair until somebody has read the chapter.**

**Nothing else moved. No device, no cost, no finding, no refusal, no chapter's place in the tide run, and the batch's spine.**

**The verification record in `state/batch-summary.md` is a list of what was checked and not a claim that nothing is wrong.** The Batch 0002 review found six hard arithmetic defects in load-bearing money and three false claims in that file's own verification section, and Batch 0003 went through three repair passes before it was closed, **so every figure taken from a state file in this batch was recomputed before it went on the page, and one of them was wrong in the batch's own card.** Read that list, not this paragraph.

## Re-entry guard for Volume 03 Batch 0005 — SUPERSEDED, and two of its claims are false

> **Read the volume close record at the top of this file first. This section is kept as history and two of its statements are wrong.**
> **1. `logs/batch-0005.review.log` does not exist and never was committed; `logs/` and `*.log` are in `.gitignore`.** So do `logs/batch-0002.review.log`, `logs/batch-0003.review.log` and `logs/batch-0004.review.log`. **The findings those passes are said to have returned survive only as prose in `state/batch-summary.md` Parts five and six. The repairs hold and were re-verified at the volume close against the chapters; the artefact does not exist.**
> **2. The controller problem flagged below as number 1, a missing `.done` marker on `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/`, is FIXED. Every prompt directory in the tree now holds a `.done` except `workspace/volume-03/volume-close/`.** Controller problem number 2, the phase ledger, is unchanged and is still off limits to an agent.
> **3. Two figures in this section are wrong: Pell Cawley is fifty-two and not fifty (Chapter 122 introduces him and says so, and Chapter 140 says it twice), and Chapter 110's *fifteen days* was one out and is now *fourteen*.**

**Volume 03 is COMPLETE. Chapters 1–150 exist as finished prose and are canon. The next phase is a VOLUME CLOSE and not a batch. The batch record is `state/batch-summary.md`; the per-chapter record is `state/chapter-summaries.md` under *Chapter 0141* to *Chapter 0150*. NO COMMITTED REVIEW FILE EXISTS FOR CHAPTERS 141–150 AND NONE MAY BE CLAIMED — the phase prompt forbade writing one into `reviews/`, and the reviewer's output for a batch of this shape is a log. Do not say a review file exists. What does exist is one external review pass over these ten chapters, run by a reviewer that wrote none of them, and it is `logs/batch-0005.review.log`; it returned five findings, all repaired, and they are `state/batch-summary.md` Part five.**

**READ THIS PARAGRAPH BEFORE THE OTHER ONE, because the batch was written and audited in four passes and each pass found things the one before it had called sound.** Chapters 141–150 and a first pass at three state files were written and checkpointed as `novel: checkpoint batch-0005`. A second run then audited that work against the chapters and found **twenty-nine defects in the prose, eight of them load-bearing, and three false claims in the first run's own verification section.** The most serious was that **Chapter 145 had the tide stone bare on a fourth — a fourth being one of the two days the stone is there** — which inverted a load-bearing canon custom and contradicted the same sentence three clauses later. **An external reviewer then read the second run's repairs and found five more, two of them blocking, and the worst of them is that the second run replaced a bad interval with a worse one in eleven places and justified it with a derivation that does not hold: the run from Chapter 137 to Chapter 145 is eight days and the second run wrote twelve, because it counted tides instead of days.** All six are repaired, and the volume-close prompt has been updated to tell the next agent to read the review log itself. **A second cold reader was then put over the whole batch to check those repairs, and it found twenty-one more: seven interval errors of the same class, a physical impossibility in Chapter 146 that a state file had been edited to hide, two tidal clocks that did not close, eight wrong facts about the world, the volume's central object described with the wrong number of women and the wrong number of hands, and a sentence that read this project's own repetition bar aloud inside the fiction.** The lists are `state/batch-summary.md` under *Verification record*, Parts two, five and six, and they are lists of what was checked and what was found and not claims that nothing is wrong. **Fifty-six defects in ten chapters over four passes, and the second pass made the batch worse while fixing nine of them, so the number is the argument for checking the chapters and not for trusting any of these files. Check the chapters.**

**What Volume 03 delivered, stated once so nothing in it has to be re-read to be known.** **A tidal breach, a link, and a revocable charter, and no monster.** On the sixth of the eighth tide the water went out through eleven yards of the middle of the seaward face of a wall nineteen years and eight months old and nobody was in it. **The batch's spine is a refusal: on the second of the ninth tide a carter asks the flat, in the lane, in the open, whether the people who go over it will stand behind a claim about those eleven yards, nobody objects, and a woman of forty-eight selling shingle says no, out loud, and is right — a party is not a silence, and a body that stands behind a record about a road is standing behind a thing nobody on that coast can stand behind.** The no costs a day. **It does not cost the person who refused; it costs a man with a cart who gave one on the fifth of the eighth tide and is not going to be asked for a second, and so that wall is not going to be sounded again this season, and the no bought that for free.** And **refusing stops nothing, and the thing refused is not destroyed by the refusal** — which Ilyan learned in Chapter 113 and carries here as a marked echo.

**And then the three, who cannot see each other and are all asked.** A woman of thirty-six with a flat boat and a towrope and eleven years of a thing nobody has asked her the name of, asked in the open at the head of a slip in four seconds, says yes and says what it costs. A man of sixty-one at a fire two miles along the coast says no first, hears it come out of his own mouth, takes four seconds, and says yes, and has wanted to be asked for two years. **A woman of fifty-one at a counter a mile and a flight of stairs up the hill says no, fast, before the ask, and then says yes to a different question in the same hour, and both of those stand and neither repairs the other.** She is not softened and going blind is not the price of anything.

**Stage 3, Witness Broker, is arrived in Chapter 147 and it is a duty and not a power.** Nothing about him is stronger. He gets no party, no bench, no body, no office and no remedy he chose alone, and nobody on that coast gives him standing. **He asks three people for permission to have their accounts put in a record; one of them says no at first, to the joining and not to the record — *if I put my words in a page with theirs then there is a page, and a page is one page* — and he does not persuade her and does not come back with it in different words. He changes the structure. Three sheets, three names, nothing at the top of any of them, no figure from one beside a figure from another.** **The whole of the profession is the separation and not the joining, and that is the thing he had been wrong about for a month and wrong about in a net loft on the third of the sixth tide.** The permanent cost is in a person: three names in a record with his own at the foot of the third sheet, **not sealed, not in a locked room, and any clerk in that building can send for it and copy it and carry the copy to a shore he has never stood on — and the finding, said once, out loud, to nobody, on a bar day, is that the broker's fee is that somebody can send for the broker's own paper.**

**The resolution is a revocable passage charter on paper, in three hands, with the three named and the burden named and the burden falling on a person and not on a harbour.** No bench sat, no council convened, nothing was printed. **A pane is a picture of one morning and cannot be crossed out; this is paper and it can be, and that is the entire reason it can be pulled.** Revocation is available to **any one person who goes over that flat** and to no body, and it may not be exercised on anybody's behalf, and it costs a day and a standing and seventy faces and no answer — worked out in five mouths by the five people who would have to pay it, **and the part that will kill it in a month, said by the boat's carpenter, is that the day a man would want to spend looking at that wall is a day he cannot use, because on a fourth, a sixth and a seventh the sea is over the crown.** The first person on the coast to think about using it is Wend Garrow, who did not build it, and he says out loud that he is not going to.

**The volume's cost to him is a relationship and not a body.** He asked a woman of twenty-four to keep three pages in about four seconds at her master's bench and did not tell her what keeping them meant, and he knew what it meant when he asked, and he asked her anyway. **She refuses, in a shed, about her own work, and she is right, and he does not repair it and writes it down on the sixth page of a penny exercise book with the date and that it was his and a reason, and the reason he put down is the reason he could stand behind in a room and it is not the reason.** **The guild is right about the wall, is right about the instruments, is in the charter, is not defeated and is not a villain, and will sound that wall at its own cost in its own season and it will not be this season. The money is not there and nobody pretends it is.**

**And the volume ends on a new question, asked by a woman of twenty-four with her hand flat on a wall, in an ordinary voice, and answered by nobody.** In the brick behind a glassmaker's bench at Thornglass there are four hundred years of weeks cut as strokes, and at the bottom of it, at the height of a knee, there is a second run of marks that is not weeks: nine uneven columns with a border on two sides and a step in it, and a row of eleven marks the same size and all different. **The System recognises it, unasked, and says nothing about what it means, and it is the first time anything in this series has recognised anything from Earth.** The signature it recognises belongs to Nadia Kestrel. **Nobody says the word that would compress the question, the System does not answer it, and it is not going to be answered in this volume. The right of refusal is not restored and the historical question is not answered and nothing on that coast is offered as either.**

**The audit, in one line: twenty-nine defects found in the first run and repaired, of which the load-bearing ones were a canon custom inverted in Chapter 145, a page count in Chapter 143 that contradicted three other chapters, an interval in Chapter 145 that the tide run does not support, two distances for the same walk in Chapters 144 and 149, and a broken sentence in the volume's closing chapter; and then five more found by an external reviewer in those repairs, of which two were blocking — an interval of twelve days where the run gives eight, written into eleven places in Chapter 145 and into three state files, a claim that she was standing on a stone that was standing bare behind her, a resettled number that did not reconcile with the premise above it, and an unlisted change to a canon rate in Chapter 141.** Two defects were found outside this batch and deliberately not repaired, because they are in closed canon: **Chapter 140 miscounts its interval from Chapter 137 by a day, and Chapters 116 and 117 give themselves days of a tide that cannot both be true.** Both are flagged in the Verification record.

So the rule for the volume close is: take nothing in these files for granted that can be checked against a chapter, and check it.***

## Where Volume 03 stands after Batch 0005 — the volume is closed

**The volume's three planned movements are delivered and none of them is a victory. The breach was carried to a claim; the claim is three sheets of paper in three hands and not one of them is joined.**

**The engine, restated at the end: a week is the only piece of time on four hundred miles of this coast that everybody agrees is a piece of time, so every list on it is a list of weeks, every one of them has holes, and there is no column anywhere on this coast for a hole.** The guild is right about the wall, is right about the instruments, is in the charter, and has not been defeated and was never a villain. **The last page of the volume is a woman of twenty-four putting a bar into a second fire, because it cannot be left once it is let in, and a boy of eleven going over at a penny, and nobody on that flat thinking anything about any of it.**

**The volume's antagonist, restated at the end: a rule that one body can apply and only local people can survive, and a record of a moving thing that does not travel, and a person who answers for nothing but what they said themselves.** Danno Ray said *a rule that cannot be changed for a year is not a rule, it is a season* on a shingle in Chapter 119 and about nine people heard it and none of them answered him, and it is referred to once in this volume, by the glassmaker, and is still not answered. **The new thing is not a new enemy. It is a category, and it is the same one in every form the volume used it: a person is not a body, and a page is one page, and a record is a thing a person has to stand behind.**

**The pattern, restated at the end: it is recognised, once, in the last chapter, by the System, and it means nothing that anybody on the page can say.** Four hundred years of weeks on a kiln wall with a gap in it about every nine years, and at the bottom of it nine uneven columns and a row of eleven marks, and a woman of twenty-four with her hand flat on the brick asking who was standing in front of the somewhere that it was copied from, and nobody answering, including the thing that has just recognised a signature four hundred years old and stopped.

**And what the volume ends on that it did not start with: three people's own words in three places, in their own mouths, not joined, and his own name at the foot of the third of them, and the record not sealed, and the finding that the broker's fee is that somebody can send for the broker's own paper.**

## Timeline

**Volume 02 ran from the twelfth day of the unloaded period to the first of the seventh month and is closed. Volume 03 opened at the sixth day of a tide on a cart road above Kestrel Reach and closed on the second of the tenth tide. The coast's calendar is a seven-day tide, bells, and the turn, and the shore calendar is not fixed by a canon chapter in this volume and must not be converted into one.**

**In Batch 0005: 141 is the seventh of the eighth tide, 142 the first of the ninth, 143 the second, 144 the third, 145 the fourth, 146 the fifth, 147 the sixth, 148 the seventh, 149 the first of the tenth, and 150 the second. Ilyan is on the coast fifty-nine days at the end of Chapter 150, and that is what the tide run gives and not a separate count. Bar days are 141, 145, 147 and 148 and the flat takes nine on each; the good days are 142, 143, 144, 146, 149 and 150 and it takes six on each.** **No weekday name is used as a date anywhere in Chapters 101–150 and none may ever be named on this coast.**

**The month-date list is unchanged and Batch 0005 added nothing to it.** Five month-dates stand in Chapters 101–110 and **one in Chapters 141–150: the third of the second month in a city four hundred miles behind him, in Chapter 150, which is the canonical date of the rendering and is a date in a city off this coast.** The eleventh month (102:76, 108:5) is still the only month named on this coast and may not be used again.

**The distances are fixed and were not moved.** The canon distances of Chapters 1–100 stand untouched. **The coast's own: the coast to Orison is four hundred miles of road; the road head is eleven miles up the coast road; Thornglass is two miles along the coast; the bar road is four miles along the coast above the north end; Wether Cross is about a mile above the harbour and the tide office is a mile and a flight of stairs up the hill from it; the guild's own office and its print shop are two hundred miles up the coast; and a school in a hundred and forty miles holds a grandmother's book.** **Three new distances were introduced by the last batch and all three are about the wall: two hundred and sixty yards long, nine hundred yards of shingle from the near stones to the far ones, four hundred yards from the head of the flat to the north end, two hundred yards of gravel, and about eleven yards of the breach.**

## Active threats

- **Eleven yards of the toe of a wall is gone and the number that says so is in a book in a shed with a date on it and nobody acted on it, and it is now seven days older, and the gravel at the top of that bar came down over the same eleven yards in Chapter 146 and a man of fifty-eight would not put a figure on it and put his heel in it and his heel did not find the bottom.** The guild will sound that wall at its own cost in its own season and it will not be this season, and the season's money is not in the arch, and it is a year, and a year ends on a letting day the tide chooses. **A shore going will take less than two winters, because this time the guild is putting stone into a hole instead of taking a wall out, and a shore going does its work on the days the sea is doing nothing else.**
- **A boy of eleven is two thousand and twenty-eight of the four thousand and sixty-eight crossings cut into a post at Wether Cross, and no figure from the crossing's money is spoken anywhere in the last ten chapters, and the margin of four has not been said aloud in a room.**
- **A woman of eighty-one is entered on a guild crossing card as the person who bears the cost of a bad week, and she has never been asked and has not been told, and in Chapter 142 he worked out where he was going and left that road off, and no person on this coast knows he did, and the decision was not un-made and was not made for him.**
- **Three people's accounts are in a record with his name at the bottom of it, and the record is not sealed and is not in a locked room, and any clerk in an office two hundred miles up this coast can send for it and copy it and carry the copy to a shore he has never stood on. He knows that and has known it since he tied the string round it.**
- **A woman of twenty-four refused to keep the three pages and she was right, and he did not repair it and wrote it down, and the reason he wrote on the page is the reason he could put his hand up to in a room and it is not the reason.**
- **A song on a coast a hundred and forty miles up says nine days and is wrong most years and dangerously wrong about one year in nine, and it is still out there and nothing in the last ten chapters touched it.**

## Active institutional pressure

**The guild is right and is not a villain and is not defeated and is not going to be defeated this season.** Its council of nine sat once, in Batch 0002, and voted five to four for a wage it cannot pay, and the four who voted no were right about the money and the five were right about the boy. **In the last ten chapters the guild said four lines by post, every word of them correct, including that it is not in dispute that the wall is nineteen years and eight months old and is to be walked, and that costs it nothing to say; a man of fifty-eight said *the guild was right* and asked for it to be said by him and in the open; and a woman of fifty-one said out loud that in four months the guild will have got a perfectly good reason to put her at a counter for the remainder of her sight, and that they will be right to, and that she will be the woman who handed them the reason.** The Slackwater assembly said no in Batch 0001 and is owed nothing and was right. The tide office is the only body on this coast that will hear anything and it hears in a room, on paper, about its own house, and its own book is a fire going to happen. The glassworks has a year of work in a cold arch and an order with a date and no figure. **A coast that has just gained its first thing ever made out of three people's own words, on paper, with a burden on a person and a revocation in the hands of anybody who crosses a flat — and the body two hundred miles up the coast has still not been told anything this week and a clerk there chose the option that writes nothing, and will choose it next quarter.**

## Active promises

**The coast's revocable passage charter EXISTS and it is paper. Stage 3, Witness Broker, IS ARRIVED and it is a duty with a consequence and not a new thing he can do to the world. The System HAS RECOGNISED something from Earth for the first time, in a Veyran record, and said nothing about what it means, and the volume's new question has been asked on the page by an ordinary person and answered by nobody.** **The right of refusal is still not restored and the historical question is still not answered and nothing on that coast was offered as either.** The unresolved promises of Volume 02 — the historical right of refusal, the authorship question and the Shale Mirror, the C—2 chain, the plate, the fifth mark, the preservation authority, the rendering, the vantage, the door, the hand, the token, the continuity line, the form with a word at the top of it, the bill and the spring ranking, the page with no successor — **are all exactly where Volume 02 left them and none of them is advanced, and the volume close must not advance them either.**

## Current relationship pressure

**Ilyan and Bettany Rook: two people who can each refuse the other correctly and neither of whom has any standing, and the volume ends with them exactly there.** He quoted her own words back to her on a bar day at the foot of a wall and told her plainly that he was quoting a woman and not a record, and she said *I have not been persuaded of anything* and meant it, and then she said yes to a different question in the same hour, unasked, and the condition she set is that it must say Bettany Rook and not an office. **She is not softened, not rescued, not redeemed, and going blind is not the price of anything here and was not used as a path to a yes. Her two panes never left her room and he never once asked her to bring one down.** They end able to disagree in public and keep working.

**Ilyan and Sorrel Gannock: eleven years of a thing nobody has asked her the name of, and he asked, in four seconds, in the open, and she said yes and said what it costs — a name at the bottom of a thing is not a wage.** Later she says no to him about the joining, out loud, in her own yard, and is right, and he does not persuade her and does not come back with it in different words. **She is the one who worked out what she would want before she would be asked to pull the charter, and she told him both because he would not ask her.**

**Ilyan and Hollis Thrale: a man of sixty-one at a fire for forty-one years who has never once been asked whether he would like to be the one standing there.** He said no first, heard it come out of his own mouth, took four seconds, and said yes. **He has had the number for two years and has not walked down the road to give it to the man who keeps the wall, and he says that is on him and not on the other man.**

**Ilyan and Sabra Trew: the volume's cost, and it is a relationship and not a body.** He asked her to keep three pages and did not tell her what keeping them meant and knew what it meant when he asked. **She said no, in a shed, about her own work, and gave the reason, and the reason is right: if she keeps them then a person in a city two hundred miles up this coast will ask her a question about one of them in four months or a year or nine years, and it will not be him, and everybody on that coast comes to her because she is the one holding it. He did not repair it. He wrote it down on the sixth page with the date and that it was his, and the reason on the page is not the reason.**

**Ilyan and Corrie Tallow: a woman of forty-eight at a stall, unpaid, four shillings and twopence a day, who refused a community out loud and was right, and who worked out what a refusal costs before anybody asked her to.** **Ilyan and Halse Tarrant: a man of fifty-eight on a wall for thirty-one years who said the guild was right and asked for it to be said by him, and who took a number on the fifth of the eighth tide and told nobody, and who is not going to be asked for a second day and is right not to be.** Ilyan and Hal Bevan: he was told what was going to be done in his name by a stranger with no standing and was not asked for a decision, and said one true useless thing about pages. Ilyan and Wend Garrow: the only man on that coast with nowhere to put a thing that has happened to him, who worked out for a day and a half that he could pull the charter and is not going to, and asked for that to go in the bundle. Ilyan and Cleve Gannock: a day given in nine words and never thanked for, and nobody has asked him for a second one. **Ilyan and Alma Gannock: he has not asked her and has not told her, and it is his, and he is still on this coast, and that is now fifty-nine days.** **Ansa Rell wrote once, by post, in Chapter 139, correctly, asking for a place where a refusal can be shown to somebody else, was refused, and is not softened and did not write again. Sera Quill is not on the coast and did not come; what she sent him is still one sheet with two questions on it, in the middle of the exercise book in his coat, and it is still the reason he understands the refusal of consent and it still did not help.**

## Current power state

**Stage 3, Witness Broker. It is a duty with a consequence and it is not a power: he cannot join accounts, cannot compel a person, cannot read a pane, cannot produce a record, has no office, no party, no bench and no body, and nobody on this coast gives him standing.** The costs are named and they are all real: **he is responsible for the safety of the three people he has linked, he is responsible for keeping their accounts apart, and the permanent cost is a permanent liability on his own record.** **He does not attack anything, he is not a hero of the water, he did not save the coast, and he got nothing he chose alone.**

**The afterimage, the left hand, the rendering, the vantage, the door and the token are all exactly where Volume 02 left them and none of them is advanced.** The rendering comes up once, in Chapter 150, at the height and the distance it has been at since the third of the second month, and it does not move, and the chapter says *it came up the way it comes up*.

**The leg was attacked twice in the last ten chapters — on the fourth of the ninth tide on wet shingle at the north end with about nine people watching, and in a shed at Thornglass with his hand flat on brick at the moment the rendering came up — and both times he told nobody. It has cleared exactly once on this coast, for about four hours, on the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass, and it has not cleared since. It is not a clock and it is not a symptom and it does not improve because a chapter needed it to, and no file in this project may say it has not cleared in a minute of the fifty-nine days.**

**Corva Silt and no clerk is still the only person who has been told he cannot read. The count of people who have worked it out is four — Corrie Tallow, Bettany Rook, Pether Voke and Belva Cray — and not one of them has ever said a word about it. He said it himself twice in the last ten chapters and nobody remarked on it either time.**

## Deliberately unresolved and not to be advanced

**The right of refusal is not restored and was not restored on the coast and is not restored by a piece of paper about a wall. The historical question is unanswered. The removed page and Sera's duplicate remain two events on two dates and her family claim is unfiled. The Bridgeward is unanswered and its road is not touched. The continuity line has been refused seven times and nobody is named and the holder has not read it. Ansa Rell is not redeemed and did not appear in the last ten chapters. The three Gannock brothers did their work and were not staged as a device and their accounts of their father's crossing were not given and may not be agreed. The pattern was recognised once by the System in the last chapter and is not explained. The Ro household is not to be called. There is no new final enemy and the planned ending is untouched, and nothing in the last ten chapters points at one.**

## Where Volume 03 stands after Batch 0001

**The volume is open and the coast is on the page. Nothing has been resolved and nothing has been secured.**

**The engine, in one sentence: a season's passage is sold for seven days a week of the tide at thirty-one shillings, it does not name the days, and it cannot be refunded on a day the water is on the bar — the chart is on a wall and free to read, the price of finding out which three days those are is a day at the crossing, and the price of not finding out is a cart.** The guild is right about the strait, is not a villain, has not been asked to be one, and has not been defeated. **A guild controlling the only safe route is a catastrophe waiting for one bad week, and the week has no name and no date, and the person who said so is the harbourmaster doing her job.**

**The batch's spine is delivered: the first refusal of consent, correct, recorded in full on the back of a fishmonger's notice, and paid for in eleven pounds of freight, two men's day, four years of a stall-holder's wanting and a boy's thirty-nine tides a week.** Slackwater's speaker said no in the open before thirty-one people, lost a tide, and said the number before he started, and was not talked out of it. **And the consequence is the finding: a tariff on a road is not in a matter about a strait and never was. The flat is not in anything and has not been in anything for four hundred years, and there is not one body on this coast that owns nine hundred yards of shingle.**

**The rate is in a new coin at a new multiple and it was found on the ground.** One penny a tide; the days the water is worst are charged what the days it is best are charged, because a tariff that says *dangerous* is a tariff that can be refused and there is nobody at that counter to argue with anybody on a bad day. A boy of eleven makes thirty-nine tides a week, three shillings and threepence, eight pounds nine a year, against a commonest household figure of threepence a week. **Thirteen times.** The washer, the bucket, the farthing, the barrel lid, the called reading, the eight-times figure, the man who sells salt and a woman who walks a long way to avoid a rate are Volume 02's and none of them is on this coast.

**The batch's real mechanism, and it is about the coast and not about a mystery: a charter that is a pane of saltglass cannot be changed for a year, and a year is a person, a fire, and a letting day the tide chooses. A copy of a pane is not a copy. A copy is a year.** The fifth pane — the one that would carry the burden clause — is on an order sheet with a date against it and no money against it, and its letting day went eleven weeks before Chapter 107, so it has not begun. **The pane's authority does not rest on the guild. It rests on the man who stood beside the arch on one morning.** The guild holds the only legible copy because it pays for the only copy, and the moment it cannot pay, the coast has no record. **And the glassmaker has a second pane of his own on the wall of his own shed that the guild has never seen, and the two do not agree — two columns out of eleven, the third and the eighth, the two that record a place and not a depth — and he cannot get at the guild's to put the two side by side and cannot be sure his own is right either, because a channel moves. He has told nobody, and then he told a stranger, and nobody on that coast knows.** That is the volume's counterpart to Corva Silt with the failure inverted, and it does **not** make the guild wrong about the water: the guild's people are right in their hands and the guild's glass is a photograph of a morning, so what is wrong is the record's age, not the harbourmaster. And because a pane cannot be crossed out, the guild is right to refuse a stall-holder's thirty-one years of pencil — **and right that a wrong thing in a pane is a rule for ever — and the same fact is how a burden line got onto a crossing card in the first place.** The crossing card and the tide-office card are printed from one plate in one shop, one under the other, with a hair's width between them, and a man who cannot read filled in one of them and copied every line on the sheet. **Alma Gannock, eighty-one, has been entered on a bar road as the person who bears the cost of a bad week, for eight years, and has never been asked, and the toll is short twice and she paid it out of her own box, and the road works and has never been shut and has not been touched. The boy in her hut is eight.**

**What he did and did not do with it.** He asked her how many carts and did not ask her what the bottom line says, and he did not tell her either, and he wrote the question he asked and the one he did not on the same page in the same hand, with the reason and the date and that it is his, and handed it to a woman with a ledger, who told him it was the most honest piece of paper she had read in six years and the least use anybody had ever made of one. **That is his to answer and it is not answered.**

**The batch close: a partial, real, paid-for change and a question, not a verdict.** A person who guides a cart over Wether Cross on a tide is not a trespasser by that fact alone and is not counted as a cart, entered on paper and not in the returns on purpose so that it can be crossed out, with no name on it on purpose. It cost an afternoon of a young man's day and an hour of a woman's bad light, and about two pounds a year in a wage out of a margin of four. Thirty-one years of pencil went onto a shelf in the paper book and **deliberately not onto the list for the fire**, because a thing that is not the record can be wrong and a thing that is the record has to be true; that cost five mornings of a stall and a half-crown of custom off a crew of eleven years. And the batch ends on the plainest question anybody on that coast has heard put, and the first in fifteen days that is not about a person at all: **what does a body do for a year?**

## Timeline

**Volume 02 ran from the twelfth day of the unloaded period to the first of the seventh month and is closed. Volume 03 opened at the sixth day of a tide on a cart road above Kestrel Reach. The coast's calendar is a seven-day tide, bells, and the turn, and the shore calendar is not fixed by a canon chapter in this volume and must not be converted into one.**

**In Batch 0001: 101 is the sixth day of a tide, 102 the seventh, 103 the first of the next, 104 the second running into the third, 105 the third, 106 the fifth and then the sixth, 107 the sixth, 108 the seventh, 109 the first of the next, and 110 the second to the sixth. Ilyan has been on the coast fifteen days at the end of Chapter 110, which is the sixth of the first tide to the sixth of the third, counted the way the chapter counts it.** **No weekday name is used as a date anywhere in Chapters 101–110 and none may be named in any later batch. All time is bells, tides and turns.**

**FIVE month-dates stand in Chapters 101–110, in three groups. The state record said three until this correction, and a count of three was wrong on its own terms.** Group one, **four instances of two dates, both Volume 02 shore-calendar back-references about a city four hundred miles off**: the rendering's third of the second month, at 104:17 and 110:53; and the leg's last clearing, the eighteenth of the fifth, at 110:51. Group two, **two instances of one date, and this is the one that is not a back-reference**: *the eleventh month*, at 102:76 and 108:5, both times the month the guild took the reading man off the crossing, four years before this batch. It is consistent in both places, it dates nothing in the batch's own present, and **it is the only month named on this coast and it may not be used again.** **The twenty-sixth of the fourth, which an earlier version of this line quoted here, is not in Chapters 101–110 at all — it is a Volume 02 date from Chapter 96 and belongs to a different volume's record.**

**The distances are fixed and were not moved.** The canon distances of Chapters 1–100 stand untouched: the ninth bridge to Halloway four, to Nethergill eleven, Halloway to Nethergill seven, the Nine Yards about fifteen from the ninth bridge, Fallowditch eleven miles from the fen road, the cut banks nineteen, Coldwater four days up the fen road, the counting-house four miles from the court. **The coast's own: the coast to Orison is four hundred miles of road and the court has no power over any of it; the road head is eleven miles up the coast road; Thornglass is two miles along the coast; the bar road is four miles along the coast above the north end; Wether Cross is about a mile above the harbour. A Reach is about four and a half miles of water and Slackwater Reach is two of them — nine miles, three hours on a tide that allows it and not one minute more. (Corrected: this line said "about four miles", which does not make two of them nine. No chapter states a Reach's width, so no prose is disturbed.)**

## Active threats

- **A rule that cannot be changed for a year is on this coast until a letting day a year off.** The third of next tide is not that date; it is the last day the money for the fifth pane can be in the glassmaker's hand and still be out of the arch in a year. The charter is a pane; the fifth pane's letting day has gone; the tariff is a penny a tide; the days are painted on a post; the seventh has been gone over twice; and the days are not in the charter and never were. The person who has the job of changing it is going blind and has told nobody.
- **A boy of eleven is on nine hundred yards of shingle nine times a day on the three worst days of the week at the best price of the week, and there is no keeper of that flat and never has been, and no body on this coast owns the flat.**
- **An eighty-one-year-old woman is entered on a guild crossing card as the person who bears the cost of a bad week and has never been asked, and the toll she keeps is short twice in eight years and she paid both out of her own box, and nobody on this coast knows that she has been deciding alone for eight years whether four carts or sixteen go over.**
- **A woman who sells rope has thirty-one years of tide in pencil and cannot get it into a record because the record is glass and glass cannot be crossed out, and the copy that was made for her is not the record and will be found in a hundred years by somebody who does not exist yet.**
- **A number Ilyan carried nine miles by water came back as a different number in a shop, and the woman it belongs to does not know, and it cannot be put back.**
- **The guild's reading man at the crossing was voted, paid, and taken off, and Bettany Rook voted for taking him off. The crossing has no reader and the post has not been counted by anybody who can say what it counts.**
- **The council has not sat. The day book line of Chapter 110 costs about two pounds a year in a wage out of a margin of four, and the harbourmaster has entered in advance that the council will not like it.**

## Active institutional pressure

**The guild is right and is not a villain and is not defeated. The Slackwater assembly is a real body of thirty-one seats that has said no and is owed nothing and was right. The tide office is the only body on this coast that will hear anything and it hears in a room, on paper, about its own house. The council has money and has not spent it. The glassworks has a year of work in a cold arch and no order on it.** The antagonist for this volume is a category with a moving thing in it: **a rule that one body can apply and only local people can survive.** It has no person in it.

## Active promises

**The coast's revocable passage charter is Batch 0005's and has not been approached. Stage 3, Witness Broker, is not reached in this batch and Ilyan is Stage 2 at the end of Chapter 110. The System recognises nothing and has recognised no Earth identifier and the volume's new question has not been put.** The unresolved promises of Volume 02 — the historical right of refusal, the authorship question and the Shale Mirror, the C—2 chain, the plate, the fifth mark, the preservation authority, the rendering, the vantage, the door, the hand, the token, the continuity line, the form with a word at the top of it, the bill and the spring ranking, the page with no successor — **are all exactly where Volume 02 left them and none of them is advanced.**

## Current relationship pressure

**Ilyan and Bettany Rook: adversarial colleagues who are both right, and the best conversation on the coast so far, and no trust.** He worked out in about nine seconds that she is the only reader of the coast's only record and said it to her face in a yard; she told him she has two seasons of sight left in that eye and has told nobody, and took the hood off and put it back on. She worked out that he cannot read in Chapter 102 and has never said a word about it. **Neither improved the other and neither is going to.** Ilyan and Corrie Tallow: a woman who refused to be a door twice, and who told him the rate on a board and then told him what a refusal costs and would not carry it for him. **Ilyan and Pether Voke: he came nine miles by water to put a matter in two places and was refused correctly by a man who lost a tide and said the number first, and he has not repaired it and has written that down.** Ilyan and Alma Gannock: **he has not asked her and has not told her and it is his.** Ilyan and Efa Lund: **she is the only person on the coast who is angry at him and she is right and she is not satisfied and she is the only one who was angry.** Ilyan and Hollis Thrale: the old man was right about his craft and wrong about what a pane is for, and he put Ilyan down off his floor and did not ask. Ilyan and Sabra Trew: she got him down a step without comment and told him about nineteen notches and a four-day gap, and he wrote an address down and told her the truth about himself. **Sera Quill is not on the coast and did not come and there is no romance beat in a hundred and ten chapters; what she sent him was one sheet with two questions on it and it was the reason he understood the refusal of consent.**

## Current power state

**Stage 2, Seam Reader, with limited standing confined to the ninth-bridge matter, a carrier's clause four hundred miles away, and no new authority of any kind. The new fact of this batch is a language limit and a cost, not a power.** The afterimage, the rendering, the vantage, the door, the left hand and the token are all exactly where Volume 02 left them. **The leg cleared once, for about four hours, on the fourth of the third tide at Thornglass, carrying an iron bar out of a fire forty times. The same four-hour limit, the same trade, the same cost, and it came back worse. It is the established practice used and not advanced. He told nobody on this coast and is not going to. Nobody on the coast knows he cannot read, and that is different from Corva Silt and allowed.**

## Deliberately unresolved and not to be advanced

**The right of refusal is not restored and was not restored on the coast. The historical question is unanswered. The removed page and Sera's duplicate remain two events on two dates and her family claim is unfiled. The Bridgeward is unanswered and its road is not touched and the coast's version in Chapter 109 is a different thing and is not its answer. The continuity line has been refused seven times and nobody is named and the holder has not read it. Ansa Rell is not redeemed and does not appear. The three Gannock brothers are named and not staged and are saved for Batch 0003. Sabra Trew's nineteen notches are a plant and nothing is recognised. The Ro household is not to be called. There is no new final enemy and the planned ending is untouched.**

## Do not repeat

**The Volume 03 Batch 0001 device list, which is now a list of scenes and not of themes.** Six filings that returned bounded reviews; five named local arrangements with separately recorded refusals; a great many rooms that refused a clean, general or universal answer; nine benches answering a question in writing; several public answers that turned out to be prices; a system whose empty rooms are all the same kind of empty; a document that cost more to make than to pay; a great many benches' own minutes being the only durable thing. **Volume 03 may not write a seventh local arrangement, another bounded interval, another room that declines to say anything, another round of written answers from a set of benches, another bench minute as the hero of a chapter, another public answer that turns out to be a price, or another fragment recovered from a facing leaf.** Nor may it re-stage: a child asked to testify; a private account filed without consent; a marker in a working record that nobody can account for; a preservation office confronted; a plate and an index compared; a foot-gate that opens the wrong way; a barrow that is a load; a handbell attached to nothing; a bell on a rope over a wheel on a pin; a burden assigned to nobody; a keeper saying he does not read a clause; the toll bar; the facing leaf; the four lines off the ninth bridge's frame.

**And from this batch, now used and closed, and not to be restaged on the coast: a seven-day licence of which three days cannot be used at all; a rate on a crossing charged the same on a bar day as on a good day; a boy of eleven's thirty-nine tides on a flat nobody owns; eleven households against seventeen crossings; a number counted in a shop off a boat; a man counting notches alone with a piece of coal; a stamp-and-seal form printed from one plate with a burden line copied off the wrong half; an old woman on a bar road paying a toll she is not charged; a song sung at a tide stone; a painted staff and a painted post; a pane in a frame that cannot be crossed out; a cold arch with no batch in it; a glassmaker told the truth about a lens; and a glassmaker's own pane on his own wall that disagrees with the guild's.**

## Where Volume 02 stands, and what it handed to Volume 03

**Volume 02 is closed and both of its resolution lines are delivered. Neither is a victory.**

**The appeal survives and is not secure.** It is in use: a page a bench can come back with, kept by a person who cannot appeal from one word of it; a man's name under a rate read aloud four times a year; a definition of what a load is; a called reading inside a quarter; a rule that any person of a district may ask for the book to be read aloud to them and is not to be asked why; a bench of three with a room of its own; and a public answer that cost four pounds fifteen for twenty-two names in four months, entered against a road fund four miles away. **None of it is a ruling. None of it would survive a district being squeezed. Four of it cost somebody an hour a day, or a day a week, or four days of a man's sweeping. One of it cost a woman a pitch at the bottom of a slipway, one cost a recordwright a shop, two rooms and a business, and one cost a man of thirty-one the only thing his body ever gave back.** A clerk of a fund four miles away has said out loud, with a date, that in the spring he will write that poor district's law below a hand under a deck, because a fund is a list of lines written in the order of what they buy and he cannot put a room above a body.

**And Sera Quill has recovered a fragment of the original Quiet Chamber terms.** Ten words with a crease in them, entered under her licence, dated and signed, on the facing leaf beside the empty slot: **[  ] OF THE ENTRY IS A DUTY OF THE WARD, AND [  ]** — one quarter of a sentence, entered as one quarter of a sentence, **not the clause, and it restores no right of anybody to answer no.** The page itself is still missing. The historical question is not answered. **Read as a duty and not as a place, the fragment says the keeping of an entry is a thing a district owes, and a thing a district owes is done by whoever is doing its work at the time — and in four miles that is a hauler of twenty-nine who cannot appeal from one word of it and who had never been told.** Chapter 70's comparison of the facing-leaf line and the page over a pump stands unamended and Chapter 94 says so on the page.

**What this volume was about, in one line.** A page is for keeping the record of a duty. Four districts in this case have duties nobody has written down, and the work goes to whoever is standing nearest, and in every case the person standing nearest is a woman who is not allowed to appeal from one word of it.

**The volume's last image was delivered.** On the first of the seventh, at the sixth bell, at the turn, about two hundred and forty people went up a mud bank in the cold, and a woman with a barrow read out a rate that has not gone up, a man's name, a woman's nine sentences, a quarter of a penny, and a quarter of a sentence with the gaps in it. **A woman of sixty-one who has walked four miles every morning for eighty-six days used a rule for the first time in the two months since it was made, was asked a question by a person who has no right to ask it, and answered it, and the answer was hers: she did not tell the district because the district does not know, because if the bench knew that a washer of sixty-one was paying a penny a day then the next time there was a hole in the fund somebody would work out where a penny a day was coming from and it would not be a person, it would be a ratepayer.** Nobody can do one single thing about it and that is correct. The chapter closes on a boy of seventeen reading out a question about a person — *whose name is the last one in a witness line on a form that nobody has to answer to* — and on nobody answering it.

## Timeline

**The case ran Days 1–20; the ruling was Day 20 and Chapter 50 was the eleventh day of the unloaded period.** Volume 02 ran from the twelfth day of the unloaded period (Chapter 51) to the **first of the seventh month** (Chapter 100). **In Batch 0004: 81 is the second of the fourth month, 82 the third, 83 the fourth to the sixth, 84 the sixth to the ninth, 85 the tenth to the thirteenth, 86 the fifteenth, 87 the sixteenth to the eighteenth, 88 the nineteenth and the twentieth, 89 the twenty-fifth, 90 the twenty-sixth to the twenty-ninth, and then the second of the fifth month in Orison. In Batch 0005: 91 is the second and third of the fifth month, 92 the fifth, 93 the sixth to the eighth, 94 the ninth to the twelfth, 95 the thirteenth to the sixteenth, 96 the seventeenth to the twenty-first, 97 the twenty-second, 98 the twenty-fourth to the twenty-seventh, 99 the twenty-eighth of the fifth month to the twenty-eighth of the sixth, and 100 the first of the seventh.** **No weekday has been named in any chapter from Chapter 41 onward, including all of Batch 0005, and none may be named in any later batch. Work in bells, hours, tides, and dates on instruments.**

**The distances are fixed: the ninth bridge to Halloway is four miles; the ninth bridge to Nethergill is eleven; Halloway to Nethergill is seven; the Nine Yards are about fifteen from the ninth bridge; Fallowditch is eleven miles from the fen road; the cut banks are nineteen miles; Coldwater is four days up the fen road. A building with no name on the door, holding the standing authority of preservation and a cold room of nineteen shelves, is four miles from the court and is not on the fen road.** The props came off the fourth bridge on the twentieth of the first month, propped the ninth bridge's west seat for a fortnight, stood in a yard for a fortnight, and went back on the eighteenth of the second month; the deck has been open in the weather since the twenty-seventh of the third month, and the four new timbers are still lying in the mud on the north bank because there is no water in the river to float a timber up to a deck, and the man who keeps that bridge is the man sitting on it. The ninth bridge's west seat came out of its fortnight on the third of the second month, so the span has carried a load at every change since. **The water rate was chalked on the barrel lid on the seventeenth of the second month, and the quarter's readings are the first of the fourth, the called reading on the nineteenth of the fourth, and the first of the seventh. The haul rate was set on the twenty-eighth of the third month. The ninth bridge's year is set in the spring and the charter will be written below the hand under the deck.** There is no figure of thirty-four days and no figure of forty-four days in this case, and neither may be used.

## Active threats

- **A poor district's law is paid for by a bridge's road fund four miles away, and in the spring the payer will rank it below a hand under a deck.** Forty-four pounds and sixteen against forty-four pounds and four shillings, twelve shillings over the line, and a hundred and five pounds in a line that cannot be moved without a minute of a bench four miles off that no bench four miles off can ask for.
- **The page has no successor, and a carrier with a duty is the only person about four hundred people can be sent for.** He was produced a third time in the sixth month and there is still no instrument in this city that says how a second carrier is sworn in.
- **A form is a habit with a seal on it and the word at the top of it takes the paper, not the clause.** The word is in nine districts, in about nine hundred and forty uncollected forms in a box in a counting-house, and on a form in Coldwater that carries another district's charter terms and therefore a man's name. A man of thirty-four already lost four months and a roof to it; a woman of thirty-eight lost a pitch.
- **The continuity line, for the tenth time, in a copy made by copying a copy, refused for the seventh time in the same words, nobody named.** The holder has still not read the line under the fifth term of the copy in her own room and nobody in this case will tell her.
- **A figure with no name on it is not a record about a person**, and there is no line anywhere in this city on which a person is paid to put a name on one. A woman of forty-four has eleven years of pay days on a slate in a man's house.
- **A rate that falls at eight times the average on the poorest household in a district has lost a customer for eighty-six days, and the loss still cannot be counted** because nobody collects buckets.
- **The fourth bridge's deck is open in the weather** and the four new timbers are in the mud, and the man who keeps that bridge is the man on it and nobody asked him.

## Active institutional pressure

**The appeal has been used and runs; the carrier's clause has been invoked three times and the third cost a district a cart, a clerk's day, a boy's day and a man's leg.** Twenty-two names, eleven places, unchanged for nineteen years except forty-two days at twenty-one. Fallowditch has withdrawn its public *no* in writing, makes its own rules with dates on them, and holds four entries it can do nothing with. The standing authority of preservation has answered four questions — three yes and one no with a reason, three times in the same hand — and **has told the recordwright to stop asking it, and she has stopped, and what she gave up is the only leverage in this city that anybody could name.** A bench of four has agreed a unit and not a price. A bench of five has printed four hundred and sixty-three forms. Nine benches have now said in public that a form is not an instrument, in rooms with no reader and about nobody.

## Active promises

Ilyan's limited standing is restored and confined to the ninth-bridge matter; he may not be cited as a permit, a schedule, a levy, an acknowledgment, or a bearer of labor, **and there is now a piece of paper in this city that cites him four hundred and sixty-three times.** The survival of the appeal as a durable right is **delivered and not secured.** Sera's recovery of a fragment of the original Quiet Chamber terms is **delivered as ten words with a crease in them.** The historical right of refusal, of which a quarter of a sentence has to be read as a duty and not a place, is **not restored.** The authorship question and the Shale Mirror, not touched. **The blackglass token and the afterimage, which are the same kind of operation, the rendering, the vantage, the room, the door, the hand, and the leg.** The C—2 chain and the standing preservation authority, which Ilyan has said three times he is not going to chase. The nine anchor positions and their missing functions.

## Current relationship pressure

**Ilyan's limited standing is confined to the ninth-bridge matter, he is a named carrier in a poor district's charter with a duty and a consequence, past the fourth mile that duty is a favour, and he has now been produced three times.** Sera Quill refused a court's own motion on her own motion's ground and told a trialwright why, and let a man with a bad hand be asked the question nobody had ever asked, and wrote down that she knows the difference between a question and a matter and that a record is what a person knows and a claim is what a person wants. Wenna Tace holds the page, cannot appeal from it, is the door for six months, read the fragment out to two hundred people on the first of the seventh, and has just had a woman's own count of her own work entered in a bench's book against her will and with her name on it. Bryn Sallow has three figures for thirty years and does not know what is in his leg, and on the first of the seventh she spoke across a flat — to a washer, not to him. Corva Silt knows he cannot read, produced him twice, refused a boy of seventeen a line in a page, and has asked five times to be relieved of a print authority and has stopped asking. **Neris Ro's right to stop everything herself stands, including from Ilyan, and on the eighteenth of the sixth he refused a question about her household in front of about nineteen people without giving her reason, and nobody pressed him.** Abel Kearn is untouched and has not used a mechanism.

## Current power state

**Stage 2, Seam Reader**, with limited standing confined to the ninth-bridge matter, a carrier's clause in a second district's charter, and a name printed four hundred and sixty-three times in one district under a word that is in nine others. **The new fact of the batch is a cost and not a power.** On the eighteenth of the fifth, carrying a notice down a road, his leg cleared for about four hours for the first time since the twenty-sixth of the fourth month, which is **twenty-two days** — *not* the "two months and nine days" that Chapter 96 line 63 says, which is a defect in that chapter and was corrected here by the Volume 02 audit — and the finding is that the paper was the weight and the woman was not; **the leg does not clear of itself and has not since the twenty-sixth of the fourth month, and that four hours on the eighteenth of the fifth is the only time it has cleared since.** His left hand is a scar at the base of his left thumb. **The token was not touched and the fourth use stands: it took the words.** The rendering is unchanged in kind and unresolved: a room, boards, a table the size of a table a hand could go on, a lamp with the wick down, a window with weather on the wrong side of it, and four people at the table, one of them about forty-four with her back to him. **The vantage has not moved. The door in it is at the same height and the same distance it has been at since the third of the second month, and Chapter 92 has him counting that at ninety days on the fifth of the fifth — that is his own count and it does not close either way (ninety-four days from the third of the second month, ninety-one from the sixth, the day Chapter 65 gives the door), so build nothing on the number. The number ninety-nine is in no chapter and may not be used.** The System translates formal records, notices, and hearing-field boundaries entered into Ilyan's open case. **Panel count for the batch: five System-rendered entries, one each in Chapters 91, 97, 98, 99 and 100, none stating a new general rule. They are plain bold lines with no blockquote marker, each introduced by a narrative line. No bold is used as emphasis in running prose or dialogue anywhere in Chapters 91–100, and every block-quoted block in them is a physical object in the fiction.**

## Deliberately unresolved and not to be advanced

**The historical right of refusal.** The authorship question and the Shale Mirror. **The rendering, the vantage, the room, the door, the hand, the leg, and the token.** The responsible person for the continuity line, ten appearances, seven refusals, and the holder has not read it. The fifth mark in the count book. **The removed Quiet Chamber page and Sera's missing duplicate, which remain two events on two dates and were kept apart on the page in Chapter 95.** Ilsa's grandmother, the woman paid off the load book, and the levy ledger's cut name, which remain three separate things. **The Bridgeward, one step further along and no further, and the volume's closing question is about a person and is not answered.** Ansa Rell, out-voted, undefeated, and not redeemed, and deliberately not used in this batch. **What a load is worth, and the fact that a unit is not a price, and the hauler's own count of her own work in a bench's book.** **And what a page is for, which is answered as a consequence and not as a remedy.**

## Do not repeat

Five devices used and closed: a child asked to testify; a private account filed without consent; a marker in a working record that nobody can account for; a preservation office confronted; a plate and an index compared. **In Batch 0005 the preservation authority is asked four questions in writing, in a building with its door open, and answers them, and no plate and no index was compared.** Five named local arrangements with separately recorded refusals: Chapters 16, 20, 28, 30, 38. **Form L-3 and the Halloway charter are the case's two texts. The three rules of the ninth, seventeenth and twenty-ninth of the fourth month are bench-made rules about a room and a book, and the entry of the twenty-second of the fifth month that a load is a barrow is a unit and not a sixth arrangement.** Several rooms that refused a clean, general, or universal answer. **Do not restage nine benches answering a question in writing, the withdrawn name on the docket board, the called reading of Chapter 88, or the aggregate of Chapter 86. Chapter 99 asks one question of one counsel and gets one answer in ten items, and references no board beyond one clause.** Do not restage the toll bar, the Lowgill slot, the foot-gate, a handbell attached to nothing, a bell over a wheel on a pin, four hundred and eleven loads on a flat, a burden assigned to nobody, a keeper saying he does not read a clause, the facing leaf as a set-piece, or the four lines off the ninth bridge's frame. Do not re-derive the haul rate, and do not put a barrow's load into a sentence about money as a quarter of anything. **Do not name a weekday.**

**The repetition bar handed on by the Volume 02 audit — this is the hard constraint on Volume 03 and it is not a list of themes, it is a list of scenes.** Chapters 1–100 already contain: six filings that returned bounded reviews or preserved-and-declined answers; five named local arrangements with separately recorded refusals; a great many rooms that refused a clean, general, or universal answer; nine benches answering a question in writing; several public answers that turned out to be prices; a system whose empty rooms are all the same kind of empty; a document that cost more to make than to pay; and a great many benches' own minutes being the only durable thing. **Volume 03 may not write a seventh local arrangement, another bounded interval, another room that declines to say anything, another round of written answers from a set of benches, another bench minute as the hero of a chapter, another public answer that turns out to be a price, or another fragment recovered from a facing leaf.** Nor may it restage: a child asked to testify; a private account filed without consent; a marker in a working record that nobody can account for; a preservation office confronted; a plate and an index compared; a foot-gate that opens the wrong way; a barrow that is a load; a handbell attached to nothing; a bell on a rope over a wheel on a pin; a burden assigned to nobody; a keeper saying he does not read a clause; the toll bar; the facing leaf; the four lines off the ninth bridge's frame. **A marked echo of a canonical sentence by a different person is permitted. A re-staging of the same beat is not.**
